### BT21-057 — Greymon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

Catalog and KB: BT21-057 is Black Lv4/Dinosaur/ADVENTURE, DP4000, play cost 4, normal Black Lv3 evolution cost 2, and alternate Lv3 Agumon-name or Lv3 ADVENTURE evolution cost 2. Its On Play and When Digivolving clauses conditionally grant one opponent Digimon the exact `[Start of Your Main Phase] This Digimon attacks.` effect until the opponent's turn ends when the player has Tai Kamiya or an ADVENTURE Tamer. Its inherited clause is Reboot. Local KB query: `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-057` (Q4561: an unaffected target may receive the gained effect, but it does not trigger while unaffected).

Clause-to-test mapping:

- `preserves both alternate Digivolution requirements and complete coverage`: exact alternate costs, levels, names/traits, full coverage, no residual clauses.
- `grants the opponent's Digimon the printed conditional start-of-main attack`: both On Play and When Digivolving IR triggers, opponent/controller/count boundary, token and duration, Tai/ADVENTURE condition, inherited Reboot metadata.
- `with Tai, grants a selected opponent Digimon a forced start-of-main attack`: public play, selected opposing Digimon, production turn lifecycle through the opponent's start of main, forced attack and security result.
- `does not grant the attack effect without Tai Kamiya or an ADVENTURE Tamer`: public play with no qualifying Tamer and no grant.
- `alternate-digivolves from Agumon for 2`: legal public alternate evolution, exact memory payment and stack transition.
- `alternate-digivolves from a non-Agumon ADVENTURE rookie for the same printed cost`: legal trait-based alternate route using ST20-10, exact memory payment.
- `grants Reboot to a realistic evolution host`: public BT21-057 to BT21-061 evolution and inherited Reboot observable on the resulting Lv5 host.
- `keeps the grant on an attack-capable unaffected opponent Digimon but does not trigger it`: public BT21-057 grant to BT19-101 ZeedMillenniummon, whose no-source continuous immunity to opponent effects is live through the opponent turn; verifies the grant remains recorded while its forced start-of-main attack does not fire. The adjacent public BT1-010 control demonstrates that the same grant produces a normal forced attack on an unprotected target.

Focused command/results: pending root execution (requested; do not infer a pass locally). Expected focused command is `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT21/BT21-057.test.ts --reporter=verbose --no-fsModuleCache`.

Score pending focused and collection gates: implementation 2/2, IR trace 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, rules/evolution proof 2/2, delivery 0/2. Remaining gate is root-run focused evidence; Q4561's unaffected-target interaction now has a public regression pending execution.

`apps/api/src/engine/effects/grantedEffectImmunity.test.ts` adds two public controls using printed BT21-057 and Tai Kamiya: an unaffected BT19-101 receives the grant but does not attack at the opponent's Main phase, while normal BT1-010 receives the same grant and attacks. The new regression remains pending root execution and depends on the shared GrantStatic target-resolution fix; no card-local production change was made.

#### Astra Q4561 execution

The original target resolver removed unaffected recipients before a triggered effect could be granted. `GrantStatic` now preserves selected unaffected recipients only for triggered-effect token grants; activation still consults immunity at the later trigger. Public attack-capable BT19-101 and ordinary-attacker controls pass in `logs/second-pass-integration-v4.log` (new mechanism 2/2, card 8/8). The affected five-file regression also passes 188/188 in `logs/progress-grant-integration-v1.log`; shared/API/web typecheck passes in `logs/typecheck-second-pass-v2.log`. Final collection gates remain pending.

#### Astra expanded public and comparative proof

Public BT21-040→BT21-057→BT21-061 evolution pays 2 then 3. Two public attacks suspend both the evolved Reboot host and a neutral control; the actual opponent unsuspend phase unsuspends only the Reboot host. Public When Digivolving grants work with an own non-Tai ADVENTURE Tamer (ST20-12), fail with an opponent ADVENTURE Tamer or own unrelated Hero Tamer, and expire at the opponent turn end. Public On Play selects exactly one of two opponent Digimon. Both exact Agumon and ToyAgumon's containing name qualify for the printed alternate, alongside the non-Agumon ADVENTURE route. Q4561's unaffected-target comparison remains covered by the earlier mechanism regression.

### BT21-058 — Snatchmon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

Catalog verification: Black level 4 Champion, 6,000 DP, Unknown/LIBERATOR, play cost 6. `[On Play][When Digivolving]` reveal the top 3 cards, add one card with `[Vemmon]` anywhere in its text, trash the rest, then optionally place up to 2 named `[Vemmon]` from trash as bottom digivolution cards of one own Digimon. Inherited `[All Turns][Once Per Turn]` triggers when any named Vemmon returns from this Digimon’s digivolution cards to deck bottom and deletes one opponent Digimon with play cost 4 or less. The module is IR-only via `registerIrCard`, full coverage, and no residual clauses.

KB Q4562 (`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-058`) defines “in its text” across names, traits, effects, inherited effects, requirements, and other printed card text.

#### Clause-to-test map

- Public On Play reveal/trash/placement: `publicly plays Snatchmon and accounts for reveal, trash, and two-card bottom placement` uses the real play intent, named Vemmon and Vemmon-in-text cards, a neutral remainder, and exact hand/trash/stack assertions. The public When Digivolving tests use a legal BT21-056 Vemmon stack and exact hand/trash deltas: `publicly resolves the identical When Digivolving reveal path from Vemmon` proves a text hit, while `publicly resolves When Digivolving with no Vemmon-text hit by trashing all three reveals` proves the no-hit public outcome. The public four-source sequence uses two real Snatchmon producers before Destromon; existing direct timing tests supplement these public paths.
- Public When Digivolving: existing legal Snatchmon stack/evolution tests establish the route; the shared On Play/When Digivolving action identity is structural.
- Full text and name boundaries: `recognizes a card whose printed effect text mentions [Vemmon]`, the direct no-match test, the public no-hit When Digivolving test, and exact-name placement assertions distinguish text matching from named placement.
- Optional up-to-two placement/refusal: existing two-card placement test and direct action proof cover the upper bound; the public path proves two-card placement. The public `publicly declines Vemmon placement and preserves the trash source` test proves refusal.
- Inherited public producer, eligible cost boundary, and Once Per Turn: `publicly builds four Vemmon and resolves two paid Zenith returns with one inherited deletion` publicly plays helper Snatchmon (6 memory), places two Vemmon under a top Vemmon, evolves it to Snatchmon (3 minus two inherited reductions = 1), places the remaining fourth named Vemmon, and evolves normally to Destromon (5 minus two fresh reductions = 3). Two BT18-092 Zenith Tamers each suspend and return two sources on the same public attack. All four named Vemmon reach the deck, only the selected cost-4 target is deleted, a second cost-4 target and cost-5 Flarerizamon survive, and the attack finishes. Destromon is the top host; the return producers are Zenith, not Destromon's inherited effect.
- Opponent-turn inherited return and Snatchmon deletion: `publicly triggers inherited Snatchmon deletion during an opponent-turn End Attack` uses a legal Galacticmon Lv6 host with Snatchmon and Destromon as inherited sources plus two named Vemmon sources, a public opponent attack, and the printed Destromon return-two cost. It asserts both Vemmon reach deck bottom, the eligible opposing play-cost-4 target is deleted by Snatchmon's inherited effect, both inherited sources remain in the stack, and the attack ends before the security check.

The earlier draft that used two opponent attacks against a top Destromon was invalid because BT21-060 must be an inherited source for this route; it was replaced with the legal Galacticmon stack above. These public proofs supplement the existing focused trigger diagnostics. Q4562 exhaustive text-field comparisons still require final review. No production change was required; no final 10/10 is awarded.

The direct reveal fixtures now use catalog Snatchmon at 6,000 DP rather than an injected DP override, and the public placement case asserts both nonqualifying revealed cards are trashed and the reveal deck is empty. Existing focused/collection checkpoint counts predate this fixture-fidelity correction and require root rerun.

The public no-hit When Digivolving regression was added after those checkpoints. It uses catalog cards only, asserts the completed reveal/trash zones, confirms no Vemmon source is placed, and verifies the printed evolution cost. Root must rerun the focused command before treating this revision as green.

#### Exact evidence

- Catalog `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`: black level-4 Appmon Digimon, play cost 5, 5000 DP, black level-3 evolution cost 2, `linkDp: 3000`, and `maxCountInDeck: 4`.
- Printed effect: App Fusion `[Watchmon] & [Savemon] & [Calendamon]: Cost 0`, Blocker, and during the owner’s turn once per turn when this Digimon gets linked, De-Digivolve 1 one opponent Digimon. Link text: `[Appmon]` trait, cost 2; When Linking De-Digivolve 1 one opponent Digimon.
- KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-059`: no card-specific entries returned.
- Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-051-060.md`, BT21-059 report. Historical 7/10 is untrusted pending focused execution.

#### Implementation audit

`apps/api/src/cards/BT21/BT21-059.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-059", compiled)`. It carries exact App Fusion names, Appmon link cost 2, Blocker, public linked-card De-Digivolve, and a self-scoped Your Turn once-per-turn linked trigger. Coverage is full with no residual clauses.

#### Clause-to-test mapping

| Clause/boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| App Fusion recipe and Appmon link cost | `preserves Blocker, App Fusion, and Appmon Link requirements` |
| Blocker keyword on linked Timemon | `de-digivolves once when Timemon itself receives a real link card` |
| Link cost, +3000 linked DP, and linked-card When Linking De-Digivolve | `links for 2, grants 3000 DP, and resolves its linked De-Digivolve` |
| Exactly one opposing Digimon is selected; another opponent Digimon and own Digimon are not affected | same test, with `preferInstanceIds` and unchanged `otherOpponent`/`ownOther` stacks |
| Invalid non-Appmon link is refused without paying cost | `refuses the paid link requirement for a non-Appmon card without spending memory` |
| Invalid same-name App Fusion material is refused atomically | `refuses same-name App Fusion material without moving either card` |
| Your Turn linked trigger from Timemon itself | `de-digivolves once when Timemon itself receives a real link card` |
| Once-per-turn boundary over two public links | `does not repeat Timemon's Your Turn linked trigger on a second public link` |
| App Fusion with two distinct available recipe names at cost 0 | `App Fuses the two available distinct recipe names through the public evolution intent` |
| Structural action target is exactly one opposing Digimon and amount 1 | `de-digivolves one opposing Digimon when this linked card links`; `de-digivolves once when Timemon itself receives a real link card` |

The behavioral cases use public `linkCard` and public `digivolve` intents, printed Appmon link material (BT21-053 Watchmon and BT21-041 Calendamon), a printed opponent stack, and actual link costs. The target-boundary case selects one of two opposing Digimon and confirms the own-side stack and second opposing stack remain unchanged. The invalid-link case confirms the Appmon requirement refuses a non-Appmon card without spending memory. The second-link case pays the second cost but confirms the once-per-turn host trigger does not De-Digivolve again. The public App Fusion declaration supplies both the Timemon hand card and exact linked partner instance, fusing the two distinct available recipe names, Watchmon and Calendamon, at cost 0; the added same-name refusal preserves both cards and memory.

### BT21-060 — Destromon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

Catalog verification: Black level 5 Ultimate Unknown/LIBERATOR Digimon, 10,000 DP, play cost 10; alternate `[Digivolve] [Vemmon]: Cost 6`. When Digivolving, until the opponent’s turn ends, opponent effects cannot trash its stacked cards; then De-Digivolve one opponent Digimon by 1 for every 2 named Vemmon in this stack. All Turns, when it would leave play, it may play one named Vemmon from its stack without paying. Inherited `[Opponent’s Turn][Once Per Turn]` returns exactly 2 named Vemmon from its stack to deck bottom to end an opponent attack. The module is IR-only via `registerIrCard`, full coverage, and no residual clauses.

KB evidence from `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-060`: Q4563 defines the stacked-card trash lock; Q4564 requires returning all 2 Vemmon for the inherited condition; Q4729 defines End Attack timing; Q4730 confirms it can end an attack from an unaffected Digimon.

#### Clause-to-test map

- Alternate Vemmon evolution/cost and scaled De-Digivolve: `publicly evolves from Vemmon for 6 and applies the scaled De-Digivolve` uses a legal BT21-056→BT21-058→BT21-060 stack and checks memory, stack, and opponent final level.
- Stack-trash lock and duration: `uses the engine's stacked-card trash lock...` and `Q4563 blocks opponent-effect stack trash...` check opponent-effect refusal versus controller-effect trash; `publicly protects its stacked cards from an opponent De-Digivolve effect` now proves the lock through a public BT21-061 play with two Tamer colors. Duration remains represented by the module action and direct timing proof.
- Public lock duration and expiry: `expires the public stacked-card lock after the opponent turn ends` publicly evolves Destromon, rejects the first opponent De-Digivolve during the protected turn, completes real turn boundaries, then proves a second public opponent De-Digivolve trashes the top Destromon card after expiry.
- Leave-play replacement: `plays one Vemmon from its stack without cost` checks the public deletion primitive, final Vemmon play, and Destromon trash.
- Inherited attack prevention, exact 2-card cost, deck-bottom zone, and refusal boundary: `returns exactly two Vemmon to end an opponent's attack before the security check` uses a public opponent attack; `Q4564 cannot pay with only one Vemmon, so the attack continues` proves insufficient-cost refusal and security continuation.
- Exact named Vemmon filtering and scaling: structural filters plus public legal stack/effect tests distinguish named Vemmon from other stacked Digimon.

Focused execution remains pending root’s serial run; the current working test count is 12. Delivery and final score remain pending collection gates, typecheck, review, and synchronization. No production change was required.

### BT21-061 — MetalGreymon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

Catalog/KB facts: Black Lv5 Cyborg/ADVENTURE, DP7000, play cost 7, normal Black Lv4 evolution cost 3; alternate Lv4 Greymon-name or Lv4 ADVENTURE evolution cost 3. On Play and When Digivolving perform one opponent De-Digivolve 1 for every two colors among the player's Tamers. During the player's turn, once per turn, when another own Digimon is played or digivolves, an ADVENTURE subject requires one own Digimon to gain Alliance for the turn, then another own Digimon may attack. Inherited Alliance is permanent. KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-061` records Q4565 (Alliance choice mandatory), Q4566 (Alliance and attacker may differ), Q4567 (attack may be declined), Q4568 (four colors means two separate De-Digivolve 1 processes), and Q4731 (the attack may still be processed when the ADVENTURE condition fails).

Clause-to-test mapping:

- `preserves both alternate Digivolution requirements and inherited Alliance`: exact alternate requirements and inherited IR.
- `keeps the conditional Alliance and optional attack inside each trigger`: both public watcher events, once-per-turn identity, mandatory Alliance and optional attack structure.
- `de-digivolves one opponent for each two Tamer colors on play`: public play, two colors, opposing target and stack boundary.
- `Q4568 performs two separate De-Digivolve 1 processes for four Tamer colors`: public alternate Digivolution into BT21-061, four distinct Tamer colors, and sequential stack result.
- `counts distinct Tamer colors rather than Tamer quantity`: public On Play with two same-color Tamers proves duplicate Tamers do not reach the second De-Digivolve process.
- `Q4565 grants Alliance mandatorily while Q4567 allows the attack to be declined`: public ADVENTURE play, mandatory Alliance, optional attack refusal, for-the-turn duration.
- `Q4731 still offers and resolves the attack after a non-ADVENTURE Digimon is played`: public non-ADVENTURE play, no Alliance grant, and completed attack/security resolution (`security.length === 0` with `!observe(engine).isAttacking()`).
- `triggers from a public ADVENTURE digivolution and separates Alliance choice from attack choice`: public ADVENTURE evolution triggers the watcher; Alliance is observed on the source, the optional watcher attack is declined, then a separate public attack uses `respondAlliance` to suspend a different ally and resolve combat.
- `independently chooses the Alliance recipient and effect attacker`: public ADVENTURE evolution grants Alliance to one recipient, then the actual optional effect attack is accepted with a different preferred attacker; security removal, suspension, attack declaration, and turn-end expiry are asserted.
- `consumes the Your Turn watcher once across two public ADVENTURE plays`: two eligible public ADVENTURE plays use distinct preferred recipients; only the first recipient gains Alliance, proving the same-source once-per-turn boundary without retargeting the first recipient.
- `grants inherited Alliance to a realistic higher evolution`: legal public BT21-057 → BT21-061 → ST15-12 stack and inherited Alliance observable on the Lv6 host.

Focused command/results: pending root execution. Expected command: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT21/BT21-061.test.ts --reporter=verbose --no-fsModuleCache`.

Scores pending root gates: implementation 2/2, IR trace 2/2, behavioral proof pending focused execution, rules/evolution proof pending focused execution, delivery 0/2. The new public tests cover Q4566's distinct Alliance/attacker choice, Q4567 refusal, Q4568 sequential De-Digivolve, and the duplicate-color boundary. No final 10/10 claim until root reruns the focused suite.

The public tests cover the own-turn watcher through real play and evolution intents, plus opponent-controller and non-ADVENTURE negatives. The new once-per-turn case performs two eligible public plays and changes the preferred recipient between them; the first gains Alliance while the second remains unchanged. A same-controller own-Digimon play/evolution while the controller is the non-active player has no legal public intent: the player action is turn-gated before this card's watcher can be distinguished. This remains an explicit delivery gap rather than a fabricated upstream negative.

### BT21-062 — Galacticmon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

Exact printed catalog and KB: Q4569/Q4570/Q4571/Q6932. When Digivolving optionally places four Vemmon-text cards from trash before using Ragnarok Cannon; Start of Main deletes an opponent Digimon; All Turns leave prevention returns four exact-name Vemmon digivolution cards.

Confirmed fix: `UseOptionWithoutCost` incorrectly skipped the placement cost when no Ragnarok Cannon existed. Existing `allowCostWithoutTarget: true` now permits that payment. Exact-name matching clarifies the separate leave-prevention cost; text matching remains only on the placement cost.

The public Snatchmon-to-Galacticmon regression has no Ragnarok Cannon anywhere, a legal Tsumemon/Vemmon/Snatchmon stack, and exactly four eligible trash cards. Acceptance moves four to bottom sources; refusal preserves trash. Both pay alternate9 minus inheritedVemmon1, memory10→2, and distinguish the evolution bonus draw. `publicly builds the legal Snatchmon stack, then protects against an opponent Option deletion` now proves the leave replacement through public evolution and public BT21-098 play. `does not treat Snatchmon text as exact Vemmon names for leave prevention` proves the exact-name boundary using four legal placement cards whose text mentions Vemmon but whose names are Snatchmon. `publicly deletes an opposing Digimon at the real start of its Main phase` exercises the Start of Main producer through `runOneTurn`/`waitForMainPhase`, with both players' decks seeded. `publicly pays all four Vemmon sources before declining an eligible Ragnarok Cannon` proves the optional refusal after the mandatory cost: two BT21-056 and two BT11-065 move from trash to the stack, memory reaches 1 from 10, and the eligible Cannon remains in hand. Supplementary tests cover using the Option when no opponent target exists, four-source prevention, partial-cost failures, normal alternate cost, and direct timing boundaries.

The earlier **11/11** and **13/13** focused results in the logs below predate the two public regressions above. Reproduce the current card after root's serialized run with: `TEST_MAX_WORKERS=1 pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT21/BT21-062.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --pool=forks --no-fsModuleCache --reporter=verbose`.

Affected `effectOptionUseCost` and `useOption` mechanism suites passed in `logs/focused-057-067-mechanisms-v1.log`. Scoped Oxfmt/Oxlint and git diff --check passed.

### BT21-063 — Gumdramon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

Catalog verification: Purple/Red level 3 Hero Digimon, 1,000 DP, play cost 3; normal level-2 evolution costs 1 in purple or red. Alternate evolution is zero cost from a level 2 with `<Save>` in its text or the Hero trait. On Play, by trashing one hand card with `<Save>` in its text or the Hero trait, draw 2. On Deletion it has `<Save>`. Inherited `[Your Turn]` +2,000 DP. The module is IR-only via `registerIrCard`, full coverage, and no residual clauses.

KB Q4572 (`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-063`) confirms “in its text” includes names, traits, effects, inherited effects, rules, and evolution/assembly requirements.

#### Clause-to-test map

- Both alternate routes and exact zero cost: `publicly uses the zero-cost Save-text alternate evolution route` uses BT12-005 Kozenimon, whose inherited text contains Save; `publicly uses the zero-cost Hero alternate evolution route` uses BT21-002 Gurimon from the breeding area (its printed Hero trait qualifies the route).
- Alternate refusal and trait/text boundary: `refuses both zero-cost alternates from neutral BT1-001, which has neither Save text nor Hero trait` preserves the card in hand and memory.
- On Play cost source and Draw 2: the Save-text and Hero public play tests use named hand cost cards and two named deck cards; the nonmatching public play test preserves the invalid card and deck.
- Save on deletion: `executes Save by placing itself under an own Tamer on deletion` proves final stack/trash zones with a legal Tamer destination.
- Public Save producer: `executes Save after a public losing battle and preserves the card under its Tamer` uses an opponent BT21-068 attack against a suspended Gumdramon and asserts the card reaches the Tamer stack rather than trash.
- Inherited +2,000 Your Turn duration: `gives its evolution host +2000 DP only during its controller's turn` checks printed host DP on both turn seats.
- Inherited +2,000 public lifecycle: `confirms inherited DP through the public owner-to-opponent turn lifecycle` runs the owner turn and opponent Main Phase with neutral decks, asserting 8000 DP during the owner turn and 6000 DP after the turn boundary.

Focused execution remains pending root’s serial run. Delivery and final scoring remain pending collection gates, typecheck, review, and synchronization. No production change was required. The On Deletion Save producer is exercised through the public engine deletion intent/verb path; the new refusal test proves the eligible optional Save can be declined after a completed public battle.

The Hero-route fixture was corrected from an illegal battle-area Digi-Egg seed to a breeding-area seed; prior checkpoint counts require rerun against this legal fixture.

### BT21-064 — Guilmon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact evidence

- Catalog `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`: purple/red level-3 Digimon, play cost 3, 1000 DP, Reptile/Hero, normal level-2 evolution cost 1 in either color, and `maxCountInDeck: 4`.
- Printed text: alternate level-2 evolution from Gigimon or a level-2 Hero at cost 0; On Play trashes one hand card with Guilmon, Growlmon, Gallantmon, or Megidramon in its name or the Hero trait to Draw 2; inherited On Deletion gains 1 memory.
- KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-064`: Q5758 and Q5759 establish the timing interaction for inherited On Deletion effects when a host and its digivolution cards are deleted; the cited rules also govern Save/deletion timing.
- Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-061-070.md`, BT21-064 report. Historical 8/10 is untrusted pending focused execution.

#### Implementation audit

`apps/api/src/cards/BT21/BT21-064.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-064", compiled)`. The IR contains both zero-cost alternate routes, the optional hand-trash/Draw 2 action with family-name-or-Hero matching, inherited On Deletion memory, full coverage, and no residual clauses.

#### Clause-to-test mapping

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Both alternate zero-cost evolution routes and inherited effect shape | `preserves both zero-cost alternate Digivolution requirements` |
| Guilmon-family hand cost and Draw 2 | `pays with a Guilmon-family card and draws exactly two` |
| Hero hand cost and Draw 2 | `pays with a Hero card and draws exactly two` |
| Optional refusal/nonmatching hand leaves card and deck unchanged | `declining the hand-trash cost does not draw`; `does not offer the hand-trash cost when the hand has no matching name or Hero trait` |
| Natural public battle deletion activates inherited +1 memory | `gains inherited memory from a public battle deletion` (public BT21-064 → BT21-068 evolution attacks suspended 6000-DP BT1-019; full `combatResolved` completion is asserted) |
| Supplemental direct deletion activation | `gains 1 memory when a realistic host carrying Guilmon is deleted` |
| Public Gigimon alternate route | `zero-cost evolves through alternate route 0` |
| Public level-2 Hero alternate route | `zero-cost evolves through alternate route 1` |
| Coverage/no residual | `preserves both zero-cost alternate Digivolution requirements` |

The positive and negative On Play cases use public `playCard` intents and named neutral deck cards, making both draws observable and accounting for the hand cost separately. The no-match case starts Guilmon in hand with only nonmatching BT1-009, and verifies the optional cost is unavailable without changing hand, trash, or deck. The public deletion case first evolves BT21-064 into catalog BT21-068, then attacks a suspended catalog BT1-019 (6000 DP); the stronger host loses, both stack cards reach trash, `combatResolved` and `isAttacking() === false` are observed, and the inherited memory gain is asserted. The direct deletion case remains supplemental. Both alternate routes use public `digivolve` intents from breeding-area catalog level-2 bases and verify zero memory expenditure. KB Q5758/Q5759's simultaneous inherited-effect ordering (including a deleted host/source leaving trash) is not separately reproduced and remains a focused gap.

### BT21-065 — Ghostmon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

Catalog/KB facts: Purple Lv3 Ghost/LIBERATOR, DP1000, play cost 3, normal Purple Lv2 evolution cost 0. During your turn, when this Digimon would digivolve into a Ghost-trait card, reduce that cost by 1. Inherited On Deletion gains 1 memory. KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-065` records Q4573 (the reduction does not trigger while this card is in breeding) and Q5334 (the reduction combines with its Hand/Main requirement-ignoring self-evolution, making that cost 2).

Clause-to-test mapping:

- `preserves complete residual-free coverage`: full IR coverage and no residual clauses.
- `reduces Ghost digivolution cost on your turn and gains memory on deletion`: exact replacement filter, amount, timing, and inherited deletion action.
- `reduces a Ghost evolution by exactly 1 through the public intent`: public battle-area Ghostmon → Ghost-trait Bakemon evolution, cost 1 and resulting memory.
- `combines Ghostmon's reduction with BT23-065's public Hand/Main evolution for cost 2 (Q5334)`: public BT23-065 Hand/Main activation places catalog Bakemon under BT21-065, then requirement-ignoring evolves that same Ghostmon into Phantomon; memory 5→3 proves the printed cost-3 action received exactly the Ghostmon reduction.
- `does not reduce an evolution into a non-Ghost card`: public non-Ghost evolution boundary, full cost 2.
- `does not reduce a Ghost evolution from the breeding area (Q4573)`: public breeding evolution boundary, full cost 2.
- `rejects a Ghost evolution during the opponent's turn before card-specific processing`: public normal-action legality rejects an evolution outside the controller's turn before card-specific replacement processing; this is supplemental turn legality evidence.
- `pays the reduced Ghost evolution from zero memory through the shared negative gauge`: public Ghost evolution at memory 0 pays the reduced remainder 1, reaches memory −1, and moves Bakemon from hand onto the stack.
- `gains 1 memory when a realistic host carrying Ghostmon is deleted`: inherited effect on a legal Bakemon/Ghostmon stack.
- `gains 1 memory when a public battle deletes a legal Ghost host stack`: public battle deletion origin, final trash/battle-area state, inherited memory gain, and completed combat predicate.

Focused command/results: pending root execution. Expected command: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT21/BT21-065.test.ts --reporter=verbose --no-fsModuleCache`. The card has no printed stack-protection clause; no stack-lock proof is claimed. Q5334 is now covered by a public cross-set producer test rather than a direct/manual trigger.

Scores pending root gates: implementation 2/2, IR trace 2/2, behavioral proof pending focused execution, rules/evolution proof pending focused execution, delivery 0/2. The BT23-065 producer is imported only by the test; BT21-065 itself remains unchanged. No final 10/10 claim.

### BT21-066 — Arresterdramon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact evidence

- Catalog `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`: purple/red level-4 Digimon, play cost 6, 6000 DP, Dragon/Hero, normal level-3 evolution cost 3, and `maxCountInDeck: 4`.
- Printed text: alternate level-3 evolution from a card with Save in its text or Hero trait for cost 2; On Play and When Digivolving may play one Hunter/Hero Tamer from hand without paying; On Deletion may place one Digimon with Save in its text or Hero trait from hand/trash under any own Tamer, then Save; DigiXros −2 with one Save-text Digimon; inherited Your Turn +2000 DP.
- KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-066`: Q4574 defines the broad “with X in its text” matcher across names, traits, effects, inherited effects, rules, and evolution/assembly requirements.
- Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-061-070.md`, BT21-066 report. Historical 8/10 is untrusted pending focused execution.

#### Implementation audit

`apps/api/src/cards/BT21/BT21-066.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-066", compiled)`. It preserves both alternate evolution routes, one Save-text DigiXros material with `maxMaterials: 1`, optional free Tamer play on both entry triggers, optional qualifying placement followed by independent optional Save, inherited Your Turn DP, full coverage, and no residual clauses.

#### Clause-to-test mapping

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Alternate requirements, DigiXros one-material cap, full coverage | `preserves both alternate Digivolution requirements, DigiXros, and complete coverage` |
| DigiXros one Save-text material and −2 cost | `DigiXroses with exactly one Save-text Digimon for a 2-memory reduction` |
| Rejecting two materials preserves candidate in hand | same test’s invalid-material assertion |
| On Play free Hunter and Hero Tamer | `plays a Hunter Tamer from hand without cost on play`; `plays a Hero Tamer from hand without cost on play` |
| When Digivolving free Hunter Tamer | `publicly plays a Hunter Tamer for free on the When Digivolving trigger` |
| Eligible free-Tamer refusal and nonmatching boundary | `publicly declines an eligible Hunter Tamer on play without moving or paying it`; `publicly declines an eligible Hero Tamer on digivolving without moving or paying it`; `does not play a nonmatching Tamer` |
| Public Hero alternate evolution at cost 2 | `publicly uses the Hero alternate evolution route at its printed cost` |
| On Deletion qualifying Save-text-only/Hero-only placement and Save self-placement | `places a Save-text-only trash card and itself under an own Tamer on deletion`; `places a Hero-only hand card and itself under an own Tamer on deletion` |
| Natural public battle deletion and completed Save placement | `publicly triggers On Deletion after losing a battle and Saves under an own Tamer` |
| Independent optional placement and Save choices after public battle deletion | `public battle deletion declines placement but accepts optional Save`; `public battle deletion accepts placement but declines optional Save`; `public battle deletion declines both choices and preserves source zones` |
| Inherited Your Turn +2000 and turn boundary | `publicly uses the Hero alternate evolution route at its printed cost` (legal public stack and 8000 DP on own turn); `gives its evolution host +2000 DP only during its controller's turn` (BT21-066 source under printed level-5 BT21-073 host: 10000 own turn, 8000 opponent turn) |

Public flows use actual play, digivolve, attack, and DigiXros intents where supported, with printed Save/Hero cards and legal Tamer destinations. The free-Tamer cases begin Arresterdramon in hand, pay its printed 6 play cost, and verify memory 10→4 while the Tamer enters without its cost; the two refusal tests decline eligible Hunter/Hero Tamer play from public On Play and When Digivolving triggers, preserving each card in hand after only the printed play/evolution cost. The DigiXros negative explicitly supplies two materials against the printed one-material cap and checks the unused material remains in hand. The natural deletion test evolves no synthetic stack: a 6000-DP Arresterdramon loses to a suspended catalog BT10-055 at 13000 DP, then full combat completion is observed before both the qualifying Hero card and deleted Arresterdramon are verified under the own Tamer. The new public battle helper captures the two independent optional prompts after completed combat and covers decline/accept combinations with exact Tamer, hand, and trash zones. Direct deletion tests isolate Save-text-only BT12-008 and Hero-only BT21-010 source branches. The inherited-duration fixture is a legal BT21-066 level-4 source under printed level-5 BT21-073 and asserts 10000 on the owner’s turn versus 8000 on the opponent’s turn.
#### Remaining gates

No score is assigned. No additional card-local production correction was required. Focused execution of the new independent public Save-choice tests is pending root; this report does not claim collection completion.

### BT21-067 — Garurumon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

Catalog facts: Purple Lv4 Beast/ADVENTURE, DP4000, play cost 4, normal Purple Lv3 evolution cost 2; alternate Lv3 Gabumon-name or Lv3 ADVENTURE evolution cost 2. Security text plays this card without paying its cost at end of battle. On Play and When Digivolving may return one own ADVENTURE Digimon from trash to hand. Inherited When Attacking, once per turn, draws 1 then trashes 1 hand card. KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-067`: no local entries.

Clause-to-test mapping:

- `preserves both alternate Digivolution requirements and residual-free coverage`: exact requirements and full IR coverage.
- `returns an ADVENTURE Digimon from the trash when played`: public play, trait target and hand/trash zones.
- `plays itself from security at end of battle without paying cost`: public security attack, `securityChecked` completion, end-of-battle placement, and no memory payment.
- `does not return a non-ADVENTURE Digimon from trash after a public play`: public On Play producer and target trait negative boundary.
- `returns an ADVENTURE Digimon from trash through a public When Digivolving trigger`: public ADVENTURE evolution producer and hand/trash transition.
- `alternate-digivolves from a non-Gabumon ADVENTURE rookie for the printed cost`: legal alternate trait route and exact cost; `refuses both alternate routes from a level-3 that is neither Gabumon nor ADVENTURE` isolates both requirement boundaries.
- `draws and trashes exactly once per turn from a realistic evolution stack`: inherited draw/trash and once-per-turn behavior.
- `retains inherited draw-trash through a legal Ghostmon-to-Garurumon stack`: public legal evolution stack and inherited attack effect; the final proof now uses a public player attack with completed combat.
- `resolves inherited draw-trash once across two same-host public attacks`: one legal blue/purple BT11-085 host carrying BT21-067 attacks, is publicly unsuspended with ST8-11 Victory Sword, then attacks again; the second attack completes without a second inherited draw, proving the once-per-turn boundary with a genuine opportunity.

Focused command/results: pending root execution. Expected command: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT21/BT21-067.test.ts --reporter=verbose --no-fsModuleCache`.

### BT21-068 — Growlmon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact evidence

- Catalog `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`: purple/red level-4 Digimon, play cost 5, 5000 DP, Dark Dragon, normal level-3 evolution cost 3, Guilmon-name alternate evolution cost 2, and `maxCountInDeck: 4`.
- Printed text: On Play and When Digivolving delete one opposing Digimon with 4000 DP or less; if this effect did not delete, trash the top 2 cards of this deck. Inherited On Deletion gains 1 memory.
- KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-068`: Q4575 requires choosing an eligible target rather than skipping deletion; Q4576 confirms a deletion-protected eligible target counts as “didn’t delete” and permits the mill branch; Q5758/Q5759 cover simultaneous inherited deletion timing with BT21-064.
- Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-061-070.md`, BT21-068 report. Historical 7/10 is untrusted pending focused execution.

#### Implementation audit

`apps/api/src/cards/BT21/BT21-068.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-068", compiled)`. Both entry triggers use the exact 4000-DP gate and `ifThisEffectDidNotDelete` mill condition; inherited On Deletion gains 1 memory; the Guilmon alternate route is present. Coverage is full with no residual clauses.

#### Clause-to-test mapping

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Exact 4000-DP eligible deletion and no mill | `must delete an eligible 4000 DP Digimon and does not mill` |
| Public On Play deletion with printed target | `deletes an eligible printed opponent through the public play intent` |
| Public When Digivolving no-target mill branch | `mills two after the public When Digivolving trigger has no eligible target` |
| Q4576 protected eligible target falls through to mill | `mills two when an eligible target prevents deletion with printed Armor Purge` |
| Over-4000 negative boundaries | `mills two when the opponent has only a 4001 DP Digimon`; same test with 9000 DP |
| Inherited On Deletion memory on a legal host stack | `gains 1 memory when a realistic host carrying Growlmon is deleted` |
| Inherited memory from a public battle deletion | `gains inherited memory when a public battle deletes a legal Growlmon stack` |
| Guilmon alternate evolution cost 2 | `uses the Guilmon alternate evolution route for exactly 2` |
| Structural trigger/action/coverage proof | `deletes an opposing Digimon and conditionally mills two`; `preserves the Guilmon alternate Digivolution requirement and inherited deletion memory` |

The positive On Play case now uses a printed 4000-DP BT1-014 target through public `playCard`; the negative When Digivolving case uses a printed BT21-045 opponent. The Q4576 case publicly plays Growlmon from hand against printed 4000-DP BT10-074 Quetzalmon with Armor Purge over a legal BT12-073 source, explicitly accepts the Armor Purge decision, waits until both named mill cards are in trash, and verifies the target survives with BT12-073 exposed. The direct timing cases retain the exact 4000 and 4001/9000 boundaries and supplement the public-origin proofs. The inherited proofs use a realistic BT21-076 host and a public battle deleting a BT21-068 host carrying Growlmon, with the memory delta asserted after battle resolution.

### BT21-069 — GulusGammamon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact source facts

- `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` identifies BT21-069 as a Purple/Red level-4 Digimon, 6000 DP, play cost 6, Dragonkin, with normal Purple/Red level-3 evolution cost 3 and alternate `[Gammamon]` evolution cost 2. The deck limit is 4.
- Printed effects: Security — at the end of the battle, play this card without paying its cost. On Play/When Digivolving — by placing one Digimon card with `[Gammamon]` in its name from hand or trash as this Digimon's bottom digivolution card, delete one opposing level-4-or-lower Digimon. Inherited: Retaliation.
- `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-069` returned no local knowledge-base entries. Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-061-070.md`, BT21-069 section; its 8/10 source score is untrusted pending current focused execution.

#### Implementation and proof mapping

`apps/api/src/cards/BT21/BT21-069.ts` has one `registerIrCard("BT21-069", compiled)` registration. Security now arms `whenSecurityBattleEnded` with a one-shot nested trigger, then plays from trash without cost and with `isSecurity:true`; this preserves the actual end-of-battle boundary. Both main triggers use an optional hand/trash bottom-source placement cost and delete exactly one opposing Digimon at level 4 or lower. The alternate Gammamon route and inherited Retaliation are represented in IR; coverage is full and residual is empty.

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Alternate `[Gammamon]` evolution cost 2 and legal/name boundary | `uses the printed alternate Gammamon evolution through a legal public stack`; `refuses the exact Gammamon alternate from %s` (Agumon and GulusGammamon negatives) |
| On Play source from hand or trash, bottom placement, and level-4 deletion | `places a Gammamon from hand and deletes an opposing level 4 or lower Digimon`; `publicly pays the On Play source from trash before deleting an eligible target` |
| When Digivolving source from trash and true bottom placement | `also pays from trash during evolution and places the card at the true bottom` |
| Optional refusal and nonmatching source refusal preserve hand/target | `does not pay or delete when the cost is declined` and its nonmatching case |
| Level-5 boundary | `may pay the optional placement cost through public %s with only a level-5 target` (On Play and When Digivolving) |
| Security free play after battle | `plays from Security only after the public security battle closes` (event asserts `securityChecked.battle` precedes `cardPlayed`) |
| Inherited Retaliation | `plays itself from security for free and grants inherited Retaliation` |
| Structural exact trigger/action/coverage checks | `preserves the Gammamon evolution route and residual-free coverage`; `uses a Gammamon bottom-stack cost to delete a level 4 or lower Digimon` |

The public evolution and security tests use real intents and settled state. The direct `fire` cases remain supplemental for source-zone and refusal boundaries. No shared engine gap is currently identified after the nested security trigger correction.

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. Catalog facts: Purple Lv4 Gossip/Appmon form, DP4000, play cost 4, normal Purple Lv3 evolution cost 2. Security: at end of battle, play this card without paying its cost. On Play and When Digivolving may return one Digimon card with the Appmon trait from trash to hand. Link requirement is Appmon trait, cost 2; When Linking may return one Appmon Digimon from trash to hand. KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-070`: no local entries.

Clause-to-test mapping:

- `preserves the Appmon link requirement and linked recovery`: exact link cost/trait and linked recovery IR.
- `plays from security and recovers Appmon`: security timing is represented through `whenSecurityBattleEnded`, both recovery triggers, full coverage and no residuals.
- `returns an Appmon from trash through its public On Play effect`: public play, Appmon target and hand/trash destination.
- `links to an Appmon for 2 and recovers an Appmon from trash`: public Link intent, exact memory cost and linked recovery.
- `recovers through the When Digivolving branch`: public legal evolution and recovery.
- `does not recover a non-Appmon Digimon`: target trait boundary.
- `publicly declines the optional On Play recovery without moving a non-Appmon card`: public refusal with unchanged trash.
- `plays itself from security without paying cost`: public security battle, end-of-battle placement, zero memory payment, and securityChecked before cardPlayed event ordering.

The new public refusal uses `autoDeclineOptional` with a non-Appmon trash card. The When Digivolving proof now asserts the printed 2-memory evolution cost, and the Security proof asserts complete combat (`isAttacking() === false`), empty Security, and `securityChecked` before `cardPlayed`.

### BT21-071 — Scopemon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact source facts

- The catalog record in `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` identifies a Purple level-4 Appmon Digimon, 4000 DP, play cost 4, with normal Purple level-3 evolution cost 2 and alternate level-3 `[Three Musketeers] in text` evolution cost 2. It has Link DP 3000, deck limit 4, and `[Link] [Appmon] trait: Cost 2`.
- Printed text: `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` by placing one card with the `[Appmon]/[Three Musketeers]` trait from hand or trash as one of your Digimon's bottom digivolution cards, gain 1 memory. `[When Linking]` Draw 2 and trash 2 cards in hand.
- `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-071` returned Q4577 (the “in its text” search scope includes names, traits, effects, inherited effects, rules, and evolution requirements) and Q4578 (a linked card is trashed when evolution causes its host to lose the Link requirement). Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-071-080.md`, BT21-071 section; its 8/10 source score is untrusted pending current focused execution.

#### Implementation and clause mapping

`BT21-071.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-071", compiled)`. The Link effect draws exactly two and trashes exactly two hand cards. Both main triggers use the optional placement cost, hand/trash source zones, bottom position, own Digimon host selector, and gain exactly one memory. The alternate Three Musketeers-text evolution and Appmon Link requirement are represented; coverage is full with no residual clauses.

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| On Play placement, host selection, and +1 memory | `places an Appmon under an own Digimon and gains memory on play` |
| Public On Play placement from trash and true bottom ordering | `places a Three Musketeers card from trash at the true bottom and gains memory` |
| Public evolution origin plus alternate cost and placement | `resolves the placement-and-memory branch from a public alternate evolution`; `uses the Three Musketeers-text alternate evolution route for 2` |
| Decline and nonmatching trait refusal | `does not place a card or gain memory when the cost is declined` and its nonmatching case |
| Public no-eligible placement boundary | `publicly declines placement when no Appmon or Three Musketeers card is eligible` |
| Public trash-source placement through legal alternate evolution | `publicly places an eligible Three Musketeers card from trash during alternate evolution` |
| Link cost, Draw 2, and exactly two hand trash | `links for 2, draws two, and trashes exactly two selected hand cards` |
| Structural effect and coverage checks | `gains memory after placing an Appmon or Three Musketeers card`; `draws 2 and trashes 2 when linked`; `keeps the evolution requirement and complete coverage metadata` |

Public `playCard`, `digivolve`, and `linkCard` intents are settled before state assertions. The true-bottom case now publicly plays Scopemon and places the catalog BT6-065 card whose `types` include Three Musketeers under a legal level-4 host over a level-3 source, proving the type-only branch and bottom insertion after the printed play payment. The public no-eligible and alternate-evolution cases retain their separate boundaries. Q4578 is recorded as a related ruling; host Link-loss behavior is engine-level and is not silently claimed as a card-local proof here.

### BT21-072 — Arresterdramon: Superior Mode

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

Catalog facts: Purple/Red Lv5 Dragon/Hero, DP10000, normal Purple or Red Lv4 evolution cost 4; alternate Lv4 with Save in its text or Lv4 Hero cost 3. It has Raid and Piercing, may attack without suspending when digivolving, gets +1000 DP for each digivolution card on all turns, and grants +2000 DP on your turn as an inherited effect. KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-072` records Q4579 (X in text includes names, traits, effects, inherited effects and requirements) and Q4580 (the When Digivolving attack may proceed while this card is suspended).

Clause-to-test mapping:

- `has Raid/Piercing, optional unsuspended attack, stack scaling, and inherited DP`: exact keywords, timing, optionality, scaling and both alternate requirements.
- `gets +1000 DP for each digivolution card and exposes Raid and Piercing`: observable legal stack count using BT21-011 → BT21-066 → BT21-072, with 14,000 owner-turn DP (10,000 + 2,000 stack scaling + 2,000 BT21-066 inherited DP), then 12,000 on the opponent turn.
- Actual Raid/Piercing outcome: `uses Raid to redirect to the highest unsuspended opponent and Piercing to check security` proves the highest target is deleted, the lower target survives, and the additional security check removes the opponent's security.
- `Q4580 attacks while already suspended without suspending again`: public legal Save-text alternate evolution from suspended BT21-066, exact cost, security attack and suspension preservation.
- `declining the optional evolution attack leaves security untouched`: optional refusal and no attack side effect.
- `gives its evolution host +2000 DP only during its controller's turn`: legal BT21-072 under printed red/yellow Lv6 BT21-045, with 14,000 DP on the owner turn and 12,000 on the opponent turn.
- `uses the Save-text/Hero alternate evolution route for 3`: both legal alternate routes and exact memory cost.
- `refuses both alternate routes from a neutral Lv4 without Save text or Hero`: public negative coverage for both requirement indexes; BT1-016 remains on the field, BT21-072 remains in hand, and memory remains 4.
- `proves inherited +2000 DP on a legal Lv4-to-Lv5 evolution stack`: public legal evolution and observable inherited DP combined with stack scaling.

The public When Digivolving attack now waits for both security resolution and `observe(engine).isAttacking() === false` before checking the suspended host and memory. The scaling fixture uses the legal BT21-011 → BT21-066 → BT21-072 chain; its 14,000 owner-turn DP includes BT21-066's inherited +2000 and falls to 12,000 on the opponent turn, isolating BT21-072's two-card scaling. The inherited fixture uses BT21-072 under legal red/yellow Lv6 BT21-045. The refusal case uses catalog BT1-016 (Lv4 Red Dinosaur, no Save text and no Hero trait), so it isolates both printed alternate filters rather than combining an unrelated turn or controller boundary.

### BT21-073 — Charismon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact source facts

- The catalog record in `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` identifies BT21-073 as a Purple/Black level-5 Appmon Digimon, 8000 DP, play cost 8, with App Fusion `[Sociamon] & [Gossipmon]: Cost 0`, normal Purple/Black level-4 evolution cost 4, and `[Link] [Appmon] trait: Cost 3` (Link DP 4000).
- Printed effects: Blocker. On Play/When Digivolving may Link one level-4-or-lower Digimon card from trash or this Digimon's digivolution cards to this Digimon without paying. During your turn once per turn, when this Digimon gets linked, give one opposing Digimon `[Start of Your Main Phase] This Digimon attacks` until their turn ends. Link effect: all turns once per turn, when this Digimon would leave the battle area, trash one of this Digimon's Link cards to prevent leaving.
- `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-073` returned Q4581 (linked candidate must have Link), Q4582 (an unaffected target can receive the effect but may fail to trigger), Q4583 (choose among Link cards when Link capacity is increased), and Q5000 (Charismon may trash itself as a Link card for prevention). Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-071-080.md`, BT21-073 section; its 8/10 source score is untrusted pending current focused execution.

#### Current accepted behavior

Only `registerIrCard("BT21-073", compiled)` registers executable behavior. Public play/evolution tests prove both eligible source zones and the free Link. The public evolution stack is BT11-075 → BT21-070 → BT21-073; earlier reports incorrectly called level-4 GulusGammamon a level-3 source. Other-host stacks, level-5 and Link-less cards are excluded.

Public Link proves cost 3 for Charismon itself and cost 2 for Gossipmon. Two separate public Links to Charismon grant the forced attack only once. The opponent's real start-of-main lifecycle proves the selected recipient attacks; unaffected-target behavior is checked against the grant immunity mechanism.

Public Gaia Force tests prove exact memory cost, Link-card choice with an increased Link limit, self-Link payment, actual optional refusal, and the once-per-turn limit while the Charismon source remains available. A public block proves printed Blocker against a weaker attacker. Public App Fusion declares the exact Sociamon/Gossipmon partner, pays zero, and leaves both materials in the evolution stack after declining the separate free Link.

The repeated Link proof exposed stale `justLinked` history: a quiet rule check left the first new Link marked for the next action, causing the later discard to lose the identity of the newest Link. Root now clears the marker when each complete rule-check cycle ends. The public two-Link regression in `engine/actions/linkHistory.test.ts` distinguishes the intended discard from the old fallback.

Executed: all 18 card assertions pass in `logs/strict-keyword-paths-v5.log`; all 472 shared mechanism assertions pass in `logs/mechanisms-top-link-identity-v1.log`. Reproduce: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT21/BT21-073.test.ts src/engine/actions/linkHistory.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --no-fsModuleCache --reporter=verbose`.

Older checkpoint history remains in the collection checkpoint file and git history. These current proofs supersede its outdated App Fusion and evolution-stack descriptions. Final collection gates and delivery remain pending; no final 10/10 is claimed.

### BT21-074 — Satellamon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact evidence

- Catalog `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`: purple/black level-5 Appmon Digimon, play cost 7, 7000 DP, normal level-4 evolution cost 4, Three Musketeers-in-text alternate evolution cost 3, Appmon link cost 3, and `maxCountInDeck: 4`.
- Printed text: On Play/When Digivolving may place one Appmon/Three Musketeers trait card from hand/trash under any own Digimon, then until the opponent’s turn ends that selected Digimon cannot be returned to hand/deck or affected by opponent De-Digivolve effects. Once per turn across When Digivolving/When Attacking, trash one Appmon/Three Musketeers trait card from an own stack to De-Digivolve one opponent by 1. When Linking deletes one opposing level-4-or-lower Digimon.
- KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-074`: Q4584 confirms broad “in its text” matching; Q4585 covers link material that ceases to meet its link requirement after evolution.
- Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-071-080.md`, BT21-074 report. Historical 7/10 is untrusted pending focused execution.

#### Implementation audit

`apps/api/src/cards/BT21/BT21-074.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-074", compiled)`. The IR binds the placement host and reuses it for both return and De-Digivolve restrictions, scopes restrictions to opponent effects, shares the once-per-turn budget between evolution and attack, carries link deletion and cost, and includes the alternate route. Coverage is full with no residual clauses.

#### Clause-to-test mapping

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Placement from hand and protection of the same own host | `places an Appmon under a Digimon and protects that Digimon` |
| Host binding and own/other-host boundary | same test, with `preferInstanceIds`, protected `host`, and unchanged `otherHost` |
| Optional placement refusal with no eligible Appmon/Three Musketeers card | `does not place or protect a host when the optional placement has no eligible card` |
| Optional placement refusal with an eligible card preserves that card | `publicly refuses the optional placement while preserving an eligible Appmon card` |
| Opponent return protection versus unprotected control | `blocks an opponent's public return on the protected host but allows it on an unprotected control` (legal level-4 BT1-019 over level-3 BT1-010; exact Option memory 10→3→−4) |
| Opponent De-Digivolve protection versus unprotected control | `blocks an opponent's public De-Digivolve on the protected host but allows it on an unprotected control` (two independent BT21-059 link producers; legal BT21-045 stacks) |
| Public When Digivolving placement and protection | `applies the same placement and protection through public When Digivolving` |
| Protection duration through opponent turn and expiry | `keeps public protection through the opponent turn and expires at its end` |
| Shared once-per-turn De-Digivolve cost across public evolution and attack | `shares the once-per-turn De-Digivolve budget from public evolution into public attack` |
| Optional refusal preserves stack material and target | `does not pay the stack-trash cost when the effect is declined` |
| Public When Attacking De-Digivolve and stack-cost trash | `uses the public attack intent to De-Digivolve an opposing stack` |
| Link cost 3, +4000 link DP, and level-4 boundary deletion | `links for 3 and deletes only the level-4 boundary target` |
| Three Musketeers alternate evolution cost 3 | `uses the Three Musketeers-text alternate evolution route for 3` |
| Full coverage and action/host binding | `protects a Digimon and shares once-per-turn De-Digivolve` |

Public flows cover play, attack, link, alternate evolution, and opponent Option/link effects with printed Appmon/Three Musketeers material and legal stacks. The public refusal case has an eligible Appmon card and uses `autoDeclineOptional`, preserving the card and leaving the host unprotected. The Cocytus Breath case uses a blue source and two public Option plays, returning the unprotected legal level-4 control while the protected host remains; each processed Option is asserted in the opponent trash and memory is checked at 3 then −4. The Timemon-link case uses two independent public Timemon producers, each with its own Appmon link card, De-Digivolving only the unprotected BT21-045 stack and leaving the protected stack intact; memory is checked at 8 then 6. The public attack case proves the cost card is removed from the Satellamon stack and the opponent stack is reduced after completed combat. The shared once-per-turn case now uses a public BT21-054 → BT21-071 → BT21-074 evolution: its first trigger trashes BT21-070 and reduces a legal BT21-063 → BT21-066 → BT21-072 opponent stack to BT21-066. A completed public player attack then leaves BT21-066 and the remaining BT21-054/BT21-071 sources unchanged, proving the second trigger cannot fire. Protection duration and the link level boundary remain observable in their public scenarios.

### BT21-075 — SkullGreymon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

Catalog facts: Purple/Red Lv5 Undead/ADVENTURE, DP8000, play cost 8, normal Purple or Red Lv4 evolution cost 4; alternate Lv4 Greymon-name or Lv4 ADVENTURE evolution cost 3. On Play and When Digivolving give one own Digimon Raid and Retaliation until the opponent's turn ends. On Deletion, and inherited On Deletion, may play one own ADVENTURE-trait card with play cost 4 or less from trash without paying its cost. KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-075`: no local entries.

Clause-to-test mapping:

- `grants Raid and Retaliation and recurs ADVENTURE`: exact trigger/action pairing, same-target constraint, duration, deletion clauses, alternate costs and coverage.
- `plays a qualifying ADVENTURE Digimon from trash when deleted`: public deletion and free ADVENTURE play.
- `grants Raid and Retaliation to the same selected Digimon`: selected target receives both keywords and self-target boundary.
- `naturally uses granted Raid to redirect a public player attack`: public On Play grants Raid to an established printed-7000-DP own Digimon, whose public attack deletes the suspended victim and leaves the opponent's security unchanged at one.
- `publicly proves granted Retaliation deletes an established host after it loses to higher DP`: public On Play grants both keywords to printed BT2-075 Myotismon; its attack into printed 13000-DP BT10-055 BloomLordmon deletes both combatants.
- `publicly normal-evolves for 4 and recovers an ADVENTURE card after battle deletion`: public BT15-011 normal evolution into BT21-075 for memory 4, then battle against printed 13000-DP BT10-055 BloomLordmon; Retaliation deletes both combatants and the On Deletion effect publicly plays BT21-057 from trash.
- `keeps play grants through the opponent turn and expires them on the next own turn`: public Main play, complete opponent-turn lifecycle, and explicit active/expired keyword assertions.
- `plays an ADVENTURE Tamer at the play-cost-4 boundary` / `does not play an ADVENTURE card above play cost 4`: exact cost boundary and negative.
- `does not cross the ADVENTURE trait boundary for a cheap non-ADVENTURE card`: public SkullGreymon attack into suspended catalog BT10-055 BloomLordmon (DP13000) deletes the 8000-DP SkullGreymon; after `!observe(engine).isAttacking()`, BT1-009 Monodramon (play cost 2, Mini Dragon) remains in the owner's trash and the BloomLordmon remains on board.
- `does not play an eligible ADVENTURE card from the opponent's trash`: the same public combat shape with opponent BT21-057 Greymon in trash proves the controller boundary; the 13000-DP BloomLordmon survives and BT21-057 remains in the opponent's trash after combat settles.
- `executes the inherited deletion play from a realistic stack`: legal BT21-057 → BT21-075 → ST6-13 evolution stack and inherited deletion play.
- `uses the Greymon-name/ADVENTURE alternate evolution route for 3`: both legal alternate routes and exact cost.

The public combat cases isolate the printed `your` and `[ADVENTURE]` restrictions with actual card identities: BT1-009 is within the cost ceiling but lacks the trait, while BT21-057 has the trait and cost but belongs to the opponent. Both assert the audited attacker is deleted, the suspended BT10-055 target survives, and combat has fully resolved before checking trash. The normal-evolution scenario uses printed BT15-011, which is neither Greymon-named nor ADVENTURE, pays the catalog normal cost 4, and naturally reaches On Deletion recovery after a real battle. Existing cost-5 and no-eligible-choice coverage remains separate. The card's two optional On Deletion effects are still asserted structurally as one qualifying play each; no once-per-turn clause is printed.

### BT21-076 — WarGrowlmon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact evidence

- Catalog `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`: purple/red level-5 Digimon, play cost 7, 7000 DP, Cyborg, normal level-4 evolution cost 4, Growlmon-name alternate evolution cost 3, and `maxCountInDeck: 4`.
- Printed text: On Play/When Digivolving trash the top 2 cards, then gain Raid and Retaliation until the opponent’s turn ends. Once per turn when attacking, may digivolve into a hand Megidramon/ChaosGallantmon, reducing that digivolution by 1 per 10 total cards in both players’ trashes. Inherited On Deletion trashes the opponent’s top security.
- KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-076`: no card-specific entries returned.
- Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-071-080.md`, BT21-076 report. Historical 7/10 is untrusted pending focused execution.

#### Implementation audit

`apps/api/src/cards/BT21/BT21-076.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-076", compiled)`. Both entry triggers mill two and grant the two keywords through opponent-turn end; the attack effect is optional, self-scoped, once per turn, and scales from both players’ trash; inherited deletion trashes opponent security; the Growlmon route is present. Coverage is full with no residual clauses.

#### Clause-to-test mapping

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Structural entry mill, keyword grants, attack evolution, inherited security | `mills two, grants keywords, and offers once-per-turn evolution` |
| Public On Play mill and Raid/Retaliation | `trashes two cards and gains Raid and Retaliation on play` |
| Retaliation battle consequence | `deletes both combatants when granted Retaliation loses to printed higher DP` |
| Granted keyword duration through opponent turn | `keeps play-granted Raid and Retaliation through the opponent turn, then expires them` |
| Attack evolution through public attack intent | `uses the public attack intent for the once-per-turn Megidramon evolution` |
| Public attack with no eligible hand target | `leaves the top card unchanged when a public attack has no eligible evolution target` |
| 0/10/20 both-trash cost reduction boundaries | `attack evolution with 0 total trash cards costs 5`; same test cases for 9, 10, and 20 |
| Once-per-turn attack evolution refusal on second attack | `uses the attack evolution only once per turn` |
| Same-turn second attack while WarGrowlmon remains on top | `keeps WarGrowlmon on top across two public attacks after Cyclonic Kick unsuspends it` |
| Inherited opponent top-security trash | `inherited deletion trashes the opponent's top security` |
| Inherited security trash from a real battle deletion | `trashes the opponent's security after a public battle deletes a legal inherited stack` (public BT21-064 → BT21-068 → BT21-076 → BT21-079 evolution sequence, printed 15,000-DP BT1-084 attacker) |
| Growlmon alternate evolution cost 3 | `uses the Growlmon alternate evolution route for 3` |

Public play, attack, and digivolve intents are represented with printed card stacks and named material. The public attack case verifies the hand Megidramon evolution pays its printed reduced cost with zero trash scaling; the no-target public attack proves the optional evolution leaves WarGrowlmon on top and does not alter hand state, waiting for `securityChecked` and the attack lifecycle to finish; the threshold table supplements it with exact 9/10/20 boundaries. Security movement is observed after a real public sequence builds Growlmon → WarGrowlmon → Megidramon, then a printed 15,000-DP BT1-084 battle deletes the suspended 13,000-DP host and the inherited effect trashes a named opponent security card. The strict once-per-turn proof builds the legal BT21-064 → BT21-068 → BT21-076 stack, declines the first optional Megidramon evolution during a public security attack, uses public BT4-108 Cyclonic Kick with BT1-089 Mimi as the green source to unsuspend the still-top WarGrowlmon, and completes a second public security attack. It asserts exact 10 − 2 − 3 − 4 = 1 memory accounting and Megidramon remaining in hand after the second opportunity. Separate public play/battle proof confirms granted Retaliation deletes both a 7,000-DP WarGrowlmon and printed 13,000-DP BT10-055 when the former loses, and a real own/opponent/own turn sequence confirms Raid and Retaliation survive through the opponent turn before expiring.
