### BT21-077 — Regulusmon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact source facts

- `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` identifies BT21-077 as a Purple/Red level-5 Digimon, 7000 DP, play cost 7, with alternate level-4 `[Gammamon] in text` evolution cost 3 and deck limit 4.
- Printed effects: On Play/When Digivolving, by trashing one hand card with `[Gammamon]` in its text, give one opposing Digimon both Collision and `[Start of Your Main Phase] This Digimon attacks` until their turn ends. On Deletion, optionally play one exact `[Canoweissmon]` or one level-4-or-lower Digimon with `[Gammamon]` in its text from trash without paying. The inherited On Deletion branch keeps only the level-4-or-lower Gammamon-text option.
- `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-077` returned Q4586 (the broad “in its text” union), Q4587 (the gained effect may be given to an unaffected Digimon but then does not trigger), and Q5001 (Collision does not activate once an unaffected target suspends). The bracket-only Canoweissmon branch uses exact name matching under the comprehensive named-card rule. Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-071-080.md`, BT21-077 section; its 7/10 source score is untrusted.

#### Implementation and clause mapping

`BT21-077.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-077", compiled)`. The main triggers require an optional hand trash cost with broad Gammamon-text matching, then select one opposing Digimon and bind both Collision and the forced start-of-main attack to that same target. Main deletion separates exact `Canoweissmon` from the level-4-or-lower Gammamon-text branch; inherited deletion excludes the exact Canoweissmon-only branch. The alternate evolution, full coverage, and empty residual are present.

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Hand cost and both effects on one selected opposing Digimon | `resolves the printed grant through a public play intent`; `trashes a Gammamon-text card and grants both effects to the same opponent` |
| Public Start of Your Main Phase attack and duration expiry | `publicly triggers the selected opponent's gained attack at their next main phase` |
| Decline and nonmatching hand-cost refusal | `does not pay or grant when the cost is declined` and its nonmatching case |
| Main On Deletion exact Canoweissmon branch | `main deletion plays Canoweissmon from trash` |
| Main level-4-or-lower Gammamon-text branch | `main deletion plays level-4 Gammamon-text from trash` |
| Public main deletion and exact Canoweissmon recovery | `publicly deletes Regulusmon in battle and plays Canoweissmon from trash` |
| Public inherited deletion and level-4-or-lower Gammamon-text recovery | `publicly deletes a host and resolves the inherited Gammamon-text recovery` |
| Inherited branch and exact Canoweissmon exclusion | `inherited deletion plays level-4 Gammamon text but not Canoweissmon` |
| Alternate legal evolution cost | `uses the level-4 Gammamon-text alternate evolution route for 3` |
| Structural same-target, exact-name, inherited, and coverage checks | `costs a Gammamon card to grant Collision and recurs on deletion` |

The central grant now has a public `playCard` origin with settled memory, hand-to-trash cost, exact target selection, and negative second-target assertion. The lifecycle proof publicly advances to the selected opponent's Main Phase, observes the gained attack, and resolves the opponent's public block decision with BT21-059 Timemon, a printed Blocker, before confirming the temporary Collision grant expires at that opponent turn's end. The two added deletion tests use completed public Digimon battles against catalog 8,000/12,000 DP blockers: one proves the main exact-Canoweissmon branch, and one proves the inherited level-4-or-lower Gammamon-text branch. Q4587/Q5001 unaffected-target behavior remains a shared engine interaction and is recorded as a rules boundary rather than claimed as an additional card-local proof.

### BT21-078 — WereGarurumon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

Catalog facts: Purple Lv5 Beastkin/ADVENTURE, DP7000, play cost 7, normal Purple Lv4 evolution cost 3; alternate Lv4 Garurumon-name or Lv4 ADVENTURE evolution cost 3. On Play and When Digivolving delete one opponent Lv4-or-lower Digimon, adding 1 to the level maximum when Tamers have at least two total colors. During your turn once per turn, another own Digimon being played or digivolving with ADVENTURE causes one own Digimon to gain Alliance for the turn, then one own Digimon may attack. Inherited Alliance is permanent. KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-078` records Q4588 (Alliance mandatory), Q4589 (Alliance and attacker may differ), Q4590 (attack optional), and Q4732 (attack may proceed when the ADVENTURE condition fails).

Clause-to-test mapping:

- `deletes level 4 or lower and triggers Alliance plus an attack`: exact deletion caps, two-color condition, watcher structure, once-per-turn and inherited metadata.
- `deletes the correct level cap without/with two Tamer colors`: public On Play level boundary and total-Tamer-color increase.
- `Q4588/Q4590 mandates Alliance for an ADVENTURE event but allows declining the attack`: public ADVENTURE play, mandatory Alliance, optional attack refusal and duration.
- `publicly uses different Digimon for Alliance and the optional attack`: public ADVENTURE play declines the watcher attack, then a separate public attack responds with Alliance using a different ally, proving Q4589.
- `publicly uses different Digimon for Alliance and the optional attack`: now waits for completed Digimon combat and asserts the suspended opponent is actually deleted after the chosen ally is used.
- `consumes the Your Turn trigger once for the same WereGarurumon source`: two public ADVENTURE plays in one turn grant Alliance only on the first trigger; the second eligible target remains without Alliance.
- `Q4732 still allows an attack when the played Digimon is not ADVENTURE`: public non-ADVENTURE event, attack resolution and no Alliance.
- `grants inherited Alliance on a realistic evolution stack`: legal BT21-067 → BT21-078 → ST6-13 stack and inherited keyword.
- `uses the Garurumon-name/ADVENTURE alternate evolution route for 3`: both legal alternate routes and exact cost.

Focused command/results: pending root execution. Expected command: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT21/BT21-078.test.ts --reporter=verbose --no-fsModuleCache`.

### BT21-079 — Megidramon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact source facts

- The catalog record in `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` identifies BT21-079 as a Purple/Red level-6 Digimon, 13,000 DP, play cost 13, with normal level-5 Purple/Red evolution cost 5 and alternate level-5 `[Growlmon]` name evolution cost 4. Deck limit is 4.
- Printed effects: Security Attack +1. End of Attack once per turn, delete all Digimon. On Deletion, optionally play one play-cost-3-or-lower Digimon with `[Guilmon]` or `[Growlmon]` in its name from trash without paying; for every 10 total cards in both players' trashes, the maximum play cost increases by 2.
- `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-079` returned no local knowledge-base entries. Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-071-080.md`, BT21-079 section; its 7/10 source score is untrusted pending current focused execution.

#### Implementation and clause mapping

`BT21-079.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-079", compiled)`. The End of Attack action targets all Digimon on both sides and is once per turn. The On Deletion action uses the Guilmon/Growlmon name filter, trash source, base play-cost ceiling 3, and a dynamic +2 per 10 cards counted across both trashes. The Security Attack keyword, Growlmon alternate route, full coverage, and empty residual are present.

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Security Attack +1 and all-Digimon End of Attack wipe | `has Security Attack +1 and deletes every Digimon on both sides at end of attack` |
| Public Security Attack +1 outcome before the once-per-turn wipe | `checks two security cards from Security Attack +1 before the End of Attack wipe` |
| Natural public attack timing for the wipe | `wipes both battle areas after a real public attack resolves` |
| End of Attack source boundary: an opponent-owned BT21-079 does not react to our unrelated attack | `does not let an opponent-owned BT21-079 wipe after our other Digimon attacks` |
| End of Attack source boundary: our BT21-079 does not react to another own Digimon's attack | `does not let its BT21-079 wipe after a different own Digimon attacks` |
| End of Attack source boundary during a losing battle with Armor Purge | `does not let an opponent BT21-079 wipe after our Armor Purge attack defeats it` |
| Base 3 ceiling and 9-card no-increase boundary | `base cost 3`; `9 total trash keeps cap 3` |
| Both-player 10-card scaling and 20-card scaling | `counts both players' trashes for the cost-5 ceiling`; `10 total trash raises cap to 5`; `20 total trash raises cap to 7` |
| Guilmon/Growlmon name matching and nonmatching refusal | `does not play a non-Guilmon-family card even below the cost ceiling` |
| On Deletion play from trash | the `main deletion plays ... from trash` cases in the ceiling table; `publicly revives a qualifying Guilmon after Megidramon loses its attack` |
| Optional On Deletion refusal and final zones | `publicly declines the optional On Deletion revival and preserves the trash card` |
| Alternate Growlmon evolution route and exact cost | `uses the level-5 Growlmon-name alternate evolution route for 4` |
| Structural target, scaling, keyword, coverage checks | `has Security Attack plus one, wipes opposing Digimon, and recurs Guilmon family` |

The public attack regression settles the battle before asserting both battle areas are deleted. The source-boundary regressions retain security so the public attack reaches its security check and end-of-attack completion; the Armor Purge case additionally proves an opponent BT21-079 remains after the attacker loses and trashes its Armor top card. Together these distinguish the printed BT21-079 attack source from a global End of Attack listener. The split-trash cost ceiling was corrected in commit `bd9d34e5d`; controller `any` counts both players. Deletion and evolution cases use production verbs and public evolution intents.

The added On Deletion cases use public attacks into suspended catalog BT1-084 Omnimon (DP15000), so catalog BT21-079 Megidramon (DP13000) is deleted in combat. They separately prove free revival of catalog BT12-007 Guilmon (play cost 3, Reptile) and optional refusal with the Guilmon instance retained in the owner’s trash; the Omnimon target remains on board in both settled states. This supplements the existing direct deletion ceiling matrix with a natural producer and explicit final-zone assertions. The public wipe regression now also requires the emitted `combatResolved` event and `!observe(engine).isAttacking()` before checking both final battle areas, so the assertions cannot pass before battle completion.

### BT21-080 — Hiro Amanokawa

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact evidence

- Catalog `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`: red/purple Hero Tamer, play cost 4, `maxCountInDeck: 4`.
- Printed text: at the start of your main phase, gain 1 memory if the opponent has a Digimon; during your turn, when an effect places digivolution cards under an own Digimon with Gammamon in its text or Hero trait, you may suspend this Tamer to Draw 1 and gain 1 memory. Security plays this card without paying its cost.
- KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-080`: Q4592 confirms broad “in its text” matching across names, traits, effects, inherited effects, rules, and evolution/assembly requirements.
- Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-071-080.md`, BT21-080 report. Historical 7/10 is untrusted pending focused execution.

#### Implementation audit

`apps/api/src/cards/BT21/BT21-080.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-080", compiled)`. It encodes the opponent-Digimon start-main condition, one combined optional suspend cost gating Draw 1 plus memory, Gammamon-text/Hero trigger filtering, Security self-play, full coverage, and no residual clauses.

#### Clause-to-test mapping

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Start-main gain with opponent present/absent | `start of main with an opposing Digimon gains 1 memory`; `start of main without an opposing Digimon gains 0 memory` |
| Gammamon-text trigger, one suspend cost, Draw 1 + memory | `suspends to draw and gain memory for a Gammamon text host` |
| Hero-trait trigger and same combined cost | `suspends to draw and gain memory for a Hero trait host` |
| Nonmatching host boundary | `does not trigger when that public placement targets a nonmatching host`; `does not trigger for a nonmatching host` (direct timing supplement) |
| Optional refusal preserves ready Tamer/deck/memory | `declining does not suspend, draw, or gain memory` |
| Security free play | `plays itself from security without paying cost` |
| Public Start of Your Main Phase lifecycle | `gains the conditional Start of Your Main Phase memory through the public turn lifecycle` |

The older direct timing helper remains supplemental after the public nonmatching-host proof.

### BT21-081 — Owen Dreadnought

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

Catalog facts: Red LIBERATOR Tamer, play cost 3. At the start of your main phase, if the opponent has a Digimon, gain 1 memory. At end of your turn, by suspending this Tamer, one own Reptile or Dragonkin Digimon gains Piercing for the turn, then that same Digimon attacks. Security plays this card without paying its cost. KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-081` records Q4593 (Piercing recipient must attack if possible) and Q4594 (a second copy cannot declare another attack during an existing attack).

Clause-to-test mapping:

- `suspends this Tamer, grants Piercing to a Reptile/Dragonkin, and makes that same Digimon attack`: exact cost, target trait union, same selection binding, temporary duration and mandatory attack shape.
- `start of main without/with opposing Digimon gains memory`: public timing condition and exact memory boundary.
- `Q4593 suspends Owen, grants Piercing, and forces the selected Digimon to attack`: public `endPhase` reaches the printed End of Your Turn timing, then asserts target selection, suspension, Piercing and attack/security outcome.
- `Q4594` simultaneous two-copy boundary: `Q4594 permits only one attack when two Owen effects trigger together` uses two public Owen End of Your Turn triggers, asserts one actual attack declaration, one security check, both Tamers suspended, and completed attack state.
- `declining does not suspend Owen or attack`: public End of Your Turn refusal and unchanged state.
- `cannot pay the suspend cost without an eligible Reptile or Dragonkin`: public End of Your Turn target eligibility refusal.
- `plays itself from security without paying cost`: public security reveal, direct Security play timing, zero memory payment, play before the securityChecked event, and no attack by the Tamer.

Focused command/results: pending root execution. Expected command: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT21/BT21-081.test.ts --reporter=verbose --no-fsModuleCache`.

Scores pending root gates: implementation 2/2, IR trace 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2 pending root execution, rules/evolution proof 2/2, delivery 0/2. No local engine gap identified; the new Q4594 public two-copy test is pending execution.

### BT21-082 — Takuya Kanbara

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact source facts

- `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` identifies BT21-082 as a red Tamer with play cost 3 and deck limit 4.
- Printed effects: Security — play this card without paying its cost. At the start of your main phase, one of your Digimon or Tamers may digivolve into a Hybrid or Hero Digimon card in hand; reduce that digivolution cost by 1 for each of your red Tamers with a different name. Inherited Your Turn once per turn — when your opponent's security stack is removed from, you may play one red Tamer card from your hand without paying its cost.
- `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-082` returned Q4595 (BT21-082 counts itself for the reduction) and Q4596 (Security effects take precedence during a security check). Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-081-090.md`, BT21-082 section; its 8/10 source score is untrusted.

#### Implementation and clause mapping

`BT21-082.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-082", compiled)`. Security is a self-only free play, with normal Security timing for a Tamer. The start-main action is a paid Digivolve from hand into a Hybrid/Hero target, retaining legal evolution requirements and applying the same-action reduction for each distinct-name red Tamer. The inherited watcher is Your Turn once per turn, gated specifically to removal from the opponent's security and limited to a red Tamer from hand.

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Security free play and no memory payment | `plays itself from security without paying cost` |
| Self-counting reduction and Hybrid evolution | `Q4595 counts itself and evolves into a Hybrid for 1 less` |
| Natural start-of-main timing and settled evolution | `resolves the reduced Hybrid evolution at the public start of main phase` |
| Distinct red Tamer names only | `counts different red Tamer names once each` |
| Non-Hybrid/non-Hero destination refusal | `does not evolve into a non-Hybrid non-Hero card` |
| Opponent security removal and free red Tamer play | `triggers the inherited Tamer play from a public opponent security check`; supplemental exact removal cases in `inherited watcher plays one red Tamer only for opponent security removal` |
| Own-security negative and once-per-turn second removal | `does not play the inherited Tamer when the opponent removes your security`; `triggers the inherited Tamer play from a public opponent security check` now performs two public removals and retains the second Tamer |
| Structural Security, paid Digivolve, trait, gate, and once-per-turn checks | `plays from security, enables paid Hybrid/Hero digivolution, and gates the inherited trigger to opponent security` |

The public start-main and attack/security tests use production intents and settled state. The inherited public test now removes two opponent security cards with separate attacks, proves the first red Tamer enters free, and proves the second same-turn opportunity does not play the second Tamer. The opponent-turn negative removes the own security card through an opponent attack and confirms no inherited play. Direct timing and production security-trash cases supplement the public origins for duplicate-name and refusal boundaries. No card-local production correction was required; Tamer Security remains immediate per the printed clause.

### BT21-083 — Taiki Kudo

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact evidence

- Catalog `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`: red/yellow Hero/Xros Heart Tamer, play cost 4, `maxCountInDeck: 4`.
- Printed text: at Start of Your Main Phase, place one hand Digimon with Xros Heart, Blue Flare, or Hero under this Tamer, then Draw 1 and gain 1 memory. During your turn, when an own Xros Heart or Hero Digimon is played or digivolves, this Tamer may suspend to make that arriving Digimon attack. Security plays this card without paying its cost.
- KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-083`: Q4597 confirms placed cards go to the bottom of the Tamer’s existing stack; Q4598 confirms a second Taiki cannot create another attack during an ongoing attack; Q4728 confirms an attack cannot be declared for a card played during End of Attack processing.
- Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-081-090.md`, BT21-083 report. Historical 8/10 is untrusted pending focused execution.

#### Implementation audit

`apps/api/src/cards/BT21/BT21-083.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-083", compiled)`. It models the three start-main placement traits, the two own-arrival trigger variants, one combined Tamer suspension cost and source-reference attack, Security self-play, full coverage, and no residual clauses.

#### Clause-to-test mapping

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Start-main placement, Draw 1, gain memory, bottom stack order | `places Xros Heart Digimon under Tamer, draws 1, gains 1 memory`; `runs the Start of Your Main Phase placement through the public turn lifecycle` |
| No qualifying hand card gate | `does NOT draw when no qualifying Digimon is in hand (canActivate gate)` |
| Structural played/digivolved watchers and Security | `registers the played/digivolved attack watcher and security skill` |
| Public Xros Heart play causes Tamer suspension and attack | `suspends to make a newly played Xros Heart Digimon attack` |
| Public Hero evolution causes Tamer suspension and attack | `suspends to make a newly digivolved Hero attack` |
| Nonmatching trait boundary | `does not suspend for a newly played nonmatching Digimon` |
| Controller boundary for the qualifying arrival watcher | `does not react when the opponent plays a qualifying Xros Heart Digimon` |
| Optional refusal preserves both cards ready | `declining the watcher leaves Taiki and the arrived Digimon unsuspended` |
| Security immediate free play | `plays itself from security without paying cost` |

Public play and digivolve flows prove both natural arrival triggers, including the arriving Digimon attack target and Tamer suspension. Those tests now wait for a `securityChecked` event and `!observe(engine).isAttacking()` before asserting the completed attack, so suspension and attack results cannot be observed mid-resolution. The new opponent-turn public play of catalog BT10-008 Shoutmon isolates the watcher’s own-controller requirement while keeping the qualifying Xros Heart trait; the existing BT1-009 play is the independent nonmatching-trait case, and both negative paths wait for arrival and a cleared pending decision before asserting no reaction. The new lifecycle test runs Start of Your Main Phase through `runOneTurn`/`waitForMainPhase`, then verifies bottom placement, draw, memory, and completion. The no-candidate and Security cases still use focused timing helpers where the testkit exposes no direct user intent for those windows. Q4598/Q4728 attack-in-progress boundaries remain rules references rather than claimed card-local proofs.

### BT21-084 — Haru Shinkai

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact evidence

- Catalog `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`: red App Driver/Appmon/Hero Tamer, play cost 4, `maxCountInDeck: 4`.
- Printed text: at Start of Your Turn, if memory is 2 or less, set it to 3. During your turn, when one of your Digimon gets linked, by suspending this Tamer Draw 1, then one of your Digimon may App Fuse into a Digimon card in hand. Security plays this card without paying cost.
- KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-084`: Q4599 confirms the linked trigger’s pending App Fusion follows the link processing window; Q5444 confirms a linked-card App Fusion during an ongoing attack cannot create a new attack and does not preserve already-lost keywords.
- Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-081-090.md`, BT21-084 report. Historical 8/10 is untrusted pending focused execution.

#### Implementation audit

`apps/api/src/cards/BT21/BT21-084.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-084", compiled)`. It encodes the memory-at-most-2 start-turn set, own linked-Digimon watcher, single Tamer suspension cost gating Draw 1, nested optional App Fusion, Security self-play, full coverage, and no residual clauses.

#### Clause-to-test mapping

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Start-of-turn memory 2→3 and 3 unchanged | `sets memory from 2 to 3 at start of turn`; remaining parameterized case; `runs the conditional Start of Your Turn memory setting through the public lifecycle` |
| Link watcher and nested App Fusion only in trigger window | `app fuses only from the linked-trigger window` |
| Public link suspends Haru, draws, and fuses | `public linking suspends Haru, draws, and app fuses the linked Digimon` |
| Optional refusal preserves Tamer/host/deck | `declining the watcher leaves Haru unsuspended and does not draw or fuse` |
| Opponent linked Digimon does not trigger own Tamer | `does not trigger for an opponent's linked Digimon` |
| Security free play and owner/zone resolution | `plays itself from security through a public attack without paying cost` |
| Structural target/cost/optional boundaries | `sets memory at the start of turn, draws on linking, and fuses from hand` |

The positive path uses a public `linkCard` intent with a legal Appmon host, named link material, a distinct draw card, and a legal App Fusion destination from hand. The refusal and opponent-link cases prove controller, optional-cost, target, and zone boundaries. The new lifecycle case runs a real own turn through `runOneTurn`/`waitForMainPhase`, proving the conditional memory set at Main Phase entry and completing the phase. The Security case now begins with a public opponent attack, waits for `securityChecked` and the attack to finish, then proves the revealed Haru moved to its owner’s battle area without changing memory and that the `cardPlayed` event followed the security check.

### BT21-085 — Davis Motomiya

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact source facts

- `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` identifies BT21-085 as a Blue/Red Tamer, play cost 4, deck limit 4.
- Printed effects: at the start of your main phase, if your opponent has a Digimon, gain 1 memory. Main: by suspending this Tamer and trashing the top stacked card of one own Armor Form Digimon, Draw 1 and gain 1 memory. Security: play this card without paying its cost.
- `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-085` returned Q4600, confirming that a once-per-turn effect remains spent after a stacked card is trashed; this ruling is a related boundary for the shared activation mechanism. Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-081-090.md`, BT21-085 section; its 8/10 source score is untrusted.

#### Implementation and clause mapping

`BT21-085.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-085", compiled)`. The start-main condition checks for an opposing Digimon. The Main Draw action has one transactional optional cost combining suspension of Davis with trashing the top card of an own Armor Form Digimon, then draws one; the following gain-memory action resolves only after that cost succeeds. The Security action is a self-only free play with normal Tamer Security timing. Coverage is full and residual is empty.

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Opponent-Digimon condition | `start of main without an opposing Digimon gains 0 memory`; `start of main with an opposing Digimon gains 1 memory` |
| Natural start-main timing | `gains the conditional memory through the public start-of-main lifecycle` |
| Suspend + top Armor Form trash cost, Draw 1, and +1 memory | `suspends, trashes only the top Armor Form source, draws, and gains memory` |
| Armor Form target boundary against another own non-Armor stack | same test, with `nonArmor` stack unchanged |
| Declining the optional cost | `declining pays neither cost and resolves neither reward` |
| No eligible Armor Form stack refusal | `cannot pay the Main effect without a stacked Armor Form Digimon` |
| Security free play and no memory payment | `plays itself from Security without paying cost` |
| Natural Security free play through a public attack | `plays itself from a public Security attack without paying cost` |
| Structural cost ordering, target trait, and Security checks | `encodes the conditional memory, paid optional Main effect, and Security play` |

The Main effect uses a public `activateEffect` intent and settles both source-zone and reward transitions, while the positive case keeps a non-Armor stack present to prove the Armor Form target boundary. The start-main regression keeps a playable card in hand, waits for the real main-phase window, asserts the memory gain before ending that phase, and retains direct timing cases for no-op/refusal boundaries. The added Security regression uses a natural player attack, asserts Security removal and complete attack resolution, and confirms Davis enters without memory payment. Q4600 is documented as a shared once-per-turn activation rule and no card-local defect is identified.

### BT21-086 — Marcus Damon

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

Catalog facts: Yellow/Red Hero Tamer, play cost 4. At the start of your main phase, if the opponent has a Digimon, gain 1 memory. On Play, one Marcus Damon may suspend. Once per turn on all turns, when this Tamer suspends, one own Digimon gains Piercing and +3000 DP for the turn, then one opponent Digimon gets -3000 DP for the turn. Security plays this card without paying its cost. KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-086`: no local entries.

Clause-to-test mapping:

- `registers the three printed timing windows and a real On Play suspension effect`: timing, target name boundary, once-per-turn watcher, security and full coverage.
- `start of main without/with opposing Digimon gains memory`: opponent Digimon condition and exact memory amount.
- `gains conditional memory through the public Start-of-Main lifecycle`: real Main-phase entry with an opposing Digimon and exact memory gain.
- `suspends a Marcus Damon on the field when played`: public On Play, exact Marcus-name target and optional suspension.
- `does not suspend an opponent's Marcus Damon`: public controller boundary using a qualifying opponent-owned Marcus in battle area.
- `grants Piercing and +3000 DP to the same Digimon, then gives an opponent -3000 DP`: public producer, selected friendly target, keyword/DP modifiers and opponent target.
- `applies Piercing in a public battle and expires the modifiers at turn end`: public On Play producer, printed 6000-DP opponent boundary, completed battle with security check, and end-of-turn removal of Piercing/+3000.
- `declining the On Play suspension leaves every Marcus unsuspended and grants no modifiers`: refusal and unchanged modifiers.
- `triggers only for this Marcus and only once per turn`: source identity and once-per-turn boundary.
- `does not repeat one Marcus watcher across public same-turn suspensions`: two public Marcus suspensions of the same source, with a public Garurumon unsuspend between them and a distinct second Digimon recipient proving the once-per-turn limit.
- `plays itself from Security without paying cost`: public security attack, zero memory payment, no Tamer attack declaration.

The added controller case plays BT21-086 from seat 0 while a separate catalog BT21-086 is already on seat 1; after the public On Play settles, the opponent-owned copy remains unsuspended. This isolates the printed `your Marcus Damon` target restriction from the existing own-copy positive. The modifier cases now use catalog BT1-009 at 3,000 DP and assert 0 DP after the printed -3,000 modifier; the public On Play modifier test explicitly awaits engine readiness. The new public battle uses printed BT1-019 at 6,000 DP so it survives the temporary -3,000 effect long enough to prove Piercing through a completed security check, then verifies the temporary modifiers expire after the real turn ends. The same-turn once-per-turn case uses public Marcus plays and BT1-036 Garurumon’s printed On Play unsuspend to create a second eligible suspension opportunity without injecting triggers.

### BT21-087 — Zenith

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact source facts

- `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` identifies BT21-087 as a Black Tamer, play cost 4, deck limit 4.
- Printed effects: at the start of your turn, if memory is 2 or less, set it to 3. On Play, reveal the top 3 cards of your deck; play one `[Vemmon]` without paying its cost or add one card with `[Vemmon]` in its text to hand, then trash the remaining revealed cards. Security: play this card without paying its cost.
- `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-087` returned Q4601, confirming the broad “with [Vemmon] in its text” scope. Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-081-090.md`, BT21-087 section; its 8/10 source score is untrusted.

#### Implementation and clause mapping

`BT21-087.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-087", compiled)`. Start of Turn uses the memory-at-most-2 condition and sets memory to exactly 3. On Play reveals exactly three, adds a broad Vemmon-text match, supports the exact Vemmon-name free-play disposition, and trashes every remainder. Security is a self-only free play with normal Tamer timing. Coverage is full and residual is empty.

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Public On Play reveal, Vemmon-text add, and remainder trash | `resolves the reveal through a public play intent`; `adds a card that mentions Vemmon and trashes every revealed remainder` |
| Exact-name Vemmon free-play disposition | `publicly chooses the exact-name Vemmon free-play disposition` |
| Exact revealed-card visibility and all three prompt candidates | `names every revealed card in the prompt that offers them` |
| No matching Vemmon-text negative | `trashes all three revealed cards when none contain Vemmon in their text` |
| Start-of-turn memory threshold | `sets memory to 3 only when it is 2 or less` |
| Natural public start-of-turn timing | `sets memory to 3 through the public start-of-turn lifecycle` |
| Security free play and On Play resolution | `plays itself from Security without paying cost and resolves its On Play reveal` |
| Structural reveal count, disposition, trash, Security, and coverage checks | `models one Vemmon-text selection with the alternative free-play destination` |

Public play, Security, and turn-lifecycle tests use production intents and settled state; the lifecycle fixture keeps a playable card in hand, waits for Main before asserting memory, and ends Main cleanly. The new public free-play case chooses an exact-name BT21-056 Vemmon, asserts it enters the battle area without cost, and confirms both remainder cards are trashed. Direct reveal cases retain explicit broad text and no-match boundaries. Q4601 is recorded as the governing text-matching rule.

### BT21-088 — Tagiru Akashi

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Source and implementation

Committed catalog `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`: purple/red Hunter/Hero Tamer, play cost 4. Q4602 defines “in its text”; Q4603 puts hand placement at the bottom under this Tamer (`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-088`).

Production registers exclusively `registerIrCard("BT21-088", compiled)`. Start Main optionally places one Save-text or Hero Digimon from hand under this Tamer, draws one and gains one memory. Your Turn optionally suspends this Tamer and transfers one card from under any own Tamer to the evolving Digimon’s bottom to reduce a Save-text or Hero evolution by one. Security plays itself free.

#### Public behavioral proof

- Five real Start Main comparisons: Save-only BT10-008 and Hero BT21-064 qualify; unrelated Digimon, Hero Tamer and Hero Digi-Egg do not. Exact candidate identity, existing bottom order, other Tamer unchanged, named draw, deck size and memory distinguish placement from refusal/nonqualification. Starting seat skips its normal first-turn draw.
- Eligible Start Main refusal preserves hand/deck and yields no memory.
- Public BT21-063 to BT21-066 evolution pays both costs and places the transferred card below the existing BT12-005 egg. Public cost negatives cover refusal, missing under-Tamer material and already suspended Tagiru.
- Public opponent-material exclusion leaves material under the opponent's Tamer untouched and charges the unreduced evolution cost.
- Public opponent-controller evolution has eligible material under an own Tamer but leaves that material and Tagiru untouched while charging the opponent's normal cost, proving the controller boundary with a real Digivolve intent.
- `supplementally leaves an own Digimon evolution unreduced while the opponent has the turn` uses the production effect-driven digivolution primitive for the otherwise turn-gated same-controller case; it charges the full alternate cost, leaves Tagiru unsuspended/material unchanged, and is explicitly supplemental rather than a public player intent.
- Public Save-only BT12-076 evolution, Hero-only BT21-013 evolution and unrelated ST6-08 evolution isolate the destination union. Material originates under a different own Tamer; assertions cover exact cost, suspension, source removal and final bottom ordering. Each normal evolution has a color/level-compatible rookie.
- Public opponent Security attack plays Tagiru with unchanged memory and completes combat.

#### Red-to-green correction

`logs/second-pass-085-088-v3.log` has 42 passing assertions and one failure: Hero-only Agunimon evolution pays full cost instead of receiving Tagiru’s reduction. `into.orFilters` was ignored by the definition predicate used for replacement registration. Replaced this card’s destination encoding with the already-supported `nameOrTrait` union (Save text OR Hero trait). No shared engine change is needed. The earlier v1/v2 failures involved invalid draw/trait fixtures and are not engine-defect evidence.

`logs/second-pass-085-088-v4.log`: **43/43 across four card files; BT21-088 16/16**. Reproduce: `TEST_MAX_WORKERS=1 pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT21/BT21-088.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --pool=forks --no-fsModuleCache --reporter=verbose`.

The 16/16 result predates the two new public boundary tests (`does not use material under an opponent's Tamer for the reduction` and `does not reduce an opponent-controller evolution`); root must rerun this card before treating the current revision as green.

#### Remaining acceptance work

The opponent-material and opponent-controller comparisons use public Digivolve intents and explicit stack, memory, suspension, and source-zone assertions. The new same-controller opponent-turn comparison uses the production effect-driven digivolution primitive because the public player action is turn-gated before Tagiru's replacement can be distinguished; it records the exact full-cost and unchanged-source outcome as supplemental evidence. Existing source-stack proof uses a seeded legal rookie/egg stack, with the destination evolution public. Final synchronized collection, typecheck/style, delivery and review gates remain pending. No 10/10 claim; authoritative scores live in ledger.json.

### BT21-089 — Takato Matsuki

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

Catalog facts: Purple/Red Hero Tamer, play cost 3. At the start of your main phase, if the opponent has a Digimon, gain 1 memory. On all turns, when any own Digimon is played or digivolves, by suspending this Tamer until the opponent's turn ends, one own Digimon with Guilmon/Growlmon/Gallantmon/Megidramon in its name or Hero trait gains Blocker and, with 10 or more total cards in both trashes, +2000 DP. Security plays this card without paying its cost. KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-089`: no local entries.

Clause-to-test mapping:

- `binds one qualifying Digimon so Blocker and conditional DP share the target`: exact name/trait filter, suspend cost, same-target binding, duration and threshold.
- `start of main without/with opposing Digimon gains memory`: opponent condition and exact memory.
- `whenPlayed/whenOneOfYoursDigivolves suspends Takato and gives selected Hero Blocker and +2000 DP`: both watcher shapes, cost, same target, keyword and ten-trash boundary; the parameterized proof splits the ten cards 5/5 across both players' trashes.
- `naturally triggers the when-played watcher from a qualifying Digimon play`: public producer and Hero target.
- `publicly declines the eligible Hero watcher without paying or granting Blocker`: public qualifying Hero play with an eligible target, explicit optional refusal, unchanged 1000 DP, and unsuspended Takato.
- `naturally triggers the digivolve watcher from a legal qualifying evolution`: public BT21-032 Veemon into BT21-036 Magnamon, using the printed Hero alternate cost 3 reduced by Veemon to 2; exact memory, source stack, inherited DP and watcher target.
- `does not treat a red Tamer-source evolution as an own Digimon evolution`: public BT7-085 red inherited-effect Tamer → BT21-013 alternate evolution; BT21-013's destination When Digivolving placement and the Q6672 bonus draw resolve, while Takato remains unsuspended and grants no Blocker.
- `does treat an ordinary Digimon-source evolution into Agunimon as an own Digimon evolution`: public BT21-032 → BT21-013 normal evolution; Takato suspends and grants Blocker.
- `expires the public Blocker and DP grant after the opponent's turn ends`: public play followed by the production turn lifecycle; Blocker and +2000 persist through the opponent's Main and clear after that turn.
- `at 9 total trash grants Blocker but not +2000 DP`: exact threshold negative.
- `accepts any own Digimon as event subject, while declining pays no cost`: event-source boundary and refusal.
- `plays itself from Security without paying cost`: public security attack, zero memory payment, completed attack state, and no Tamer attack declaration.

### BT21-090 — The Strongest of Brothers

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Sources and implementation

The exact committed catalog record is a red Option costing 3. Main reveals three, adds one Gammamon-text card, bottoms the rest, and places itself in the battle area. An own field Digimon or Tamer with Gammamon in its text waives color requirements. Its All Turns source-placement event invokes reactive Delay; the optional payload evolves one own Digimon into a Gammamon-text Digimon from hand without paying. Security optionally plays a Gammamon-text card costing at most four from hand or trash, then places this Option. KB Q4604 confirms broad text matching; Q4733 includes breeding in the field.

The sole registration is `registerIrCard("BT21-090", compiled)`. Root corrected a split watcher/manual-payload implementation into one intrinsic reactive Delay watcher. The watcher requires effect-driven source placement under an own Digimon. The shared generated effects record was synchronized using the BT21-scoped script; no shared engine change was needed.

#### Reproduced correction and public proof

`logs/second-pass-090-delay-red-099-v2.log` reproduces the old implementation leaving the aged Option unconsumed after a real eligible placement. The corrected module passes all ten assertions in `logs/second-pass-009-090-v11.log`.

- Public BT17-086 Mind Link places a source on the same turn and preserves the Option.
- Two public BT21-022 Canoweissmon plays place identified sources: the first is barred by Delay's placement-turn restriction, while the second follows actual turn aging and triggers free evolution. Final hand, trash, memory, top card, and source-only stack are asserted.
- `does not react to an ordinary public Gammamon evolution that places its source by rule` ages the Option, publicly evolves BT21-010 Gammamon into BT21-019 BetelGammamon, and proves the old Gammamon is the only source while the Option remains in the battle area and the eligible BT21-022 remains in hand. This distinguishes rule-required source placement from an effect-driven placement.
- `does not react to an opponent's public Canoweissmon source placement` ages the Option, publicly plays opposing BT21-022 Canoweissmon with BT21-010 as its bottom source, and proves the owner’s Option, host, and eligible BT21-019 hand card remain unchanged.
- The outer-refusal test first accepts the actual source-placement cost, verifies placement, then declines a distinct Delay optional prompt. The Option and eligible evolution card remain available; memory reflects the Canoweissmon play alone.
- Public Main proves reveal/add/bottom placement and the own breeding text waiver. Missing and opposing field cards do not waive color.
- Complete opponent attacks into Security prove both hand and trash free-play sources and mandatory Option placement.
- Public Security refusal and cost boundary: `Security may decline an eligible Gammamon-text play and still places the Option` preserves an eligible BT21-010 in hand after an explicit refusal, while `Security does not offer a Gammamon-text card above the printed cost-4 limit` preserves the catalog BT21-022 (play cost 7) in hand.
- Structural assertions supplement controller/provenance filters and confirm that the free evolution is nested inside the reactive watcher.

### BT21-091 — Spirit Evolution!

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact evidence

- Catalog `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`: red Option, play cost 3, Ten Warriors, `maxCountInDeck: 4`.
- Printed text: while you have a Tamer with inherited effects, ignore this card’s color requirements. Main: by trashing one hand Hybrid card, Draw 2, then place this card in the battle area. All Turns: when an own Tamer with inherited effects is played, gain Delay; Delay may digivolve one Tamer into a hand Hybrid Digimon without paying cost. Security: may play one inherited-effect Tamer from hand/trash without paying, then add this card to hand.
- No card-specific KB result was returned by `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-091`; standard Main/Delay/Security timing rules apply.
- Historical comparison: no trusted single-card report was found in this checkout; prior range claims are treated as untrusted pending focused execution.

#### Implementation audit

`apps/api/src/cards/BT21/BT21-091.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-091", compiled)`. It separates the inherited-Tamer color waiver, Main Hybrid cost/draw/placement, All Turns inherited-Tamer watcher and Delay free evolution, and Security optional play plus self return. Coverage is full with no residual clauses.

#### Clause-to-test mapping

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Main Hybrid hand cost, Draw 2, and forced battle-area placement after payment | `pays a Hybrid from hand, draws 2, and places itself in the battle area` |
| Optional refusal preserves Hybrid hand card, deck, and battle area | `declining the Hybrid cost neither draws nor places the option` |
| Inherited-Tamer color waiver with own/opponent/unrelated-Tamer boundaries | `waives red color while an own non-red Tamer has an inherited-effect source`, `does not waive color from an opponent inherited-effect Tamer`, `does not waive color from an unrelated own Tamer without inherited effects` |
| All Turns inherited-Tamer watcher and same-turn boundary | `does not resolve a same-turn Delay trigger from a public inherited-effect Tamer play` |
| Aged public Delay refusal preserves Option and Hybrid | `declines an aged public Delay trigger without disposing the Option or Hybrid` |
| Aged public Delay acceptance, Option disposal, Tamer-under-Hybrid stack, and free evolution | `accepts an aged public Delay trigger as free evolution over the inherited Tamer` |
| Security optional free Tamer play from hand/trash and self return to hand | `Security may play an inherited-effect Tamer from trash, then adds itself to hand`; `publicly plays an eligible inherited-effect Tamer from hand during Security` |
| Public Security producer, eligible refusal, and completed zero-cost resolution | `publicly resolves Security by attacking into Spirit Evolution!`; `publicly declines the eligible inherited-effect Tamer Security play` |
| Structural separation of watcher/Delay and Main/Security actions | `keeps the inherited-Tamer watcher separate from the Delay digivolution payload` |

The Main positive and refusal cases use public card play with a printed Hybrid cost card and named draw cards. The color waiver comparison uses a green BT7-089 Tamer with printed inherited effects, an opposing copy, and an unrelated own black Tamer. Delay is proved only through the printed public `whenPlayed` producer: same-turn BT21-082 play leaves the Option and Hybrid unchanged, while later-turn public BT21-082 play produces an optional prompt. The refusal path responds to that prompt and preserves the Option, Hybrid, and exact paid Tamer memory. The acceptance path uses the same aged public producer and asserts the Option is trashed, BT17-011 is placed over BT21-082, and only the printed Tamer play cost changes memory because the evolution is free. The Security tests produce the effect through public opponent attacks, cover both hand and trash sources, explicitly decline an eligible optional play, and require a `securityChecked` event plus `!observe(engine).isAttacking()` before checking final zones.

| Catalog/rules | IR trace | Behavioral proof | Peer/stack proof | Delivery gates |

| 2/2 | 2/2 | 2/2 pending focused execution | 2/2 | 0/2 pending root’s focused, mechanism, collection, typecheck, catalog-sync, review, and delivery gates |

The public Delay proofs now use reactive `whenPlayed` production and distinguish same-turn non-resolution, optional refusal, and accepted aged evolution. The accepted path asserts the printed free cost and legal Tamer-under-Hybrid stack. Focused rerun is pending root; this report does not claim collection completion.

Catalog facts: Red Xros Heart Option, play cost 2. While you have a Xros Heart Digimon, its color requirement may be ignored. Main: place all Digimon cards from one of your Xros Heart Digimon's digivolution cards under one of your Tamers, then optionally play one Xros Heart Digimon from hand with play cost reduced by 1 per card placed. Security optionally plays one Xros Heart card with play cost 5 or less from hand or trash without paying its cost. KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-092` records Q4606 (source-card order may be chosen) and Q4607 (new cards go to the bottom of existing Tamer sources).

Clause-to-test mapping:

- `encodes stack transfer, counted reduction, color waiver, and Security play`: exact IR actions, bottom/any ordering, tracked count, waiver and coverage.
- `moves only Digimon source cards under a Tamer and reduces the played card by that count`: public Main, mixed source filtering, destination order and exact memory accounting.
- `plays an eligible Xros Heart card at its unreduced cost when no source cards are available`: public Main with a legal Xros Heart host lacking digivolution cards, zero counted reduction, and exact 4-cost play after the Option cost.
- `keeps the mandatory placed source but preserves an eligible hand card when the optional play is declined`: public mandatory placement followed by optional refusal, with source moved under the Tamer, candidate retained in hand, and only the Option cost paid.
- `waives color with a Xros Heart Digimon and rejects the option without one`: color waiver condition and negative play boundary.
- `does not borrow an opponent's Xros Heart trait for the color waiver`: controller boundary for the continuous waiver, with the option remaining in hand after the refused play.
- `Security plays a cost-5 Xros Heart Tamer from trash without paying cost`: public security attack, hand/trash source, play-cost boundary and zero memory payment.
- `publicly plays an eligible Xros Heart card from hand during Security` and `publicly declines an eligible Xros Heart card during Security and preserves its source`: public hand-source acceptance, optional refusal, retained trash card, and completed security battle.
- `does not play a Xros Heart card above the Security play-cost ceiling`: public Security resolution with a real cost-9 Xros Heart card and an explicit no-play assertion.
- `honors a public reverse source-order choice and places the selected cards at the bottom`: Q4606 ordering choice and Q4607 bottom placement with an existing Tamer source.

### BT21-093 — Raging Serpentine

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact source facts

- `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` identifies BT21-093 as a red LIBERATOR Option, play/use cost 8, deck limit 4.
- Printed effects: when this card would be used, if the opponent has 3 or fewer security cards, reduce the use cost by 4. Main: delete one opposing highest-DP Digimon, then place this card in the battle area. All Turns: when the opponent's security stack is removed from, gain Delay; Delay allows one own Reptile/Dragonkin Digimon to digivolve into a Reptile/Dragonkin Digimon card in hand without paying.
- Security: delete one opposing Digimon with the highest DP. `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-093` returned Q4608, confirming Security effects take precedence in simultaneous security-check timing. Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-091-100.md`, BT21-093 section; its 7/10 source score is untrusted.

#### Implementation and clause mapping

`BT21-093.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-093", compiled)`. The use-cost modifier is hand-resident and conditionally reduces cost by 4 at opponent security count 3 or lower. Main and Security each select one opposing highest-DP Digimon; Main then places itself in the battle area. The All Turns watcher is restricted to opponent security removal and grants Delay; the separate Delay payload is optional, free, and requires both own host and hand destination to have Reptile/Dragonkin traits. Coverage is full and residual is empty.

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Main highest-DP deletion and post-effect battle-area placement | `deletes the opponent's highest-DP Digimon and lands in the battle area` |
| Public Main play origin | same public `playCard` test |
| Use-cost 8 with 3-security reduction to 4 | `with 3 opposing security pays a use cost of 4` |
| Four-security no-reduction cost 8 | `with 4 opposing security pays a use cost of 8` |
| Opponent-security removal arms Delay | `arms reactive Delay from two public security attacks and free-evolves a legal host` |
| Delay host/destination trait boundaries | same test verifies eligible Reptile/Dragonkin host and leaves ineligible host unchanged |
| Security highest-DP deletion | `Security deletes only one opposing highest-DP Digimon` |
| Public Security attack and highest-DP deletion | `deletes the highest-DP Digimon from a public Security attack` |
| Aged eligible Delay refusal and preserved zones | `declines an eligible aged Delay evolution after a public security attack` |
| Structural cost, target, Delay, Security, and coverage checks | `models the conditional use reduction, highest-DP deletion, and Delay digivolution` |

The Delay regression now uses two public attacks into an opponent's two-card security stack. The first security removal occurs on the placement turn and confirms the option remains in play with no evolution; after complete production turn aging, the second public security attack triggers the intrinsic reactive Delay, trashes the option, and free-evolves the legal BT21-017 Reptile host into BT21-025 Dragonkin. The fixture uses legal red Lv.3-to-Lv.4 stacks, an ineligible Hybrid host, and populated decks so lifecycle resolution can settle normally. Q4608 is recorded as the governing Security-order ruling.

The added Security test uses a public opponent attack and asserts the printed highest-DP target reaches trash while the attacker survives and the Security attack completes. The added refusal test publicly plays and ages the Option, then presents an eligible BT21-017 → BT21-025 Delay evolution opportunity after a later security removal and explicitly declines it, preserving the Option, host, destination, and remaining security.

### BT21-094 — Armor Digivolution

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

Catalog facts: Blue Option, play cost 3. Main reveals the top 3 cards, adds one card with Davis Motomiya in its name and one Free-trait card among them to hand, trashes the rest, then places this card in the battle area. All turns, when the top stacked card of any own Armor Form Digimon is trashed, reactive Delay optionally trashes this Option and allows one own Digimon to digivolve into an Armor Form Digimon in hand without paying the cost. Security activates this card's Main effect. KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-094`: no local entries.

Clause-to-test mapping:

- `adds the Davis Motomiya and Free cards from the reveal, trashes the rest`: public Main reveal, exact three-card processing, matching hand destinations, filler trash, option battle-area placement, and the printed 3-memory payment before resolution.
- `keeps the Armor Form trash watcher separate from its Delay payload`: exact `whenDigimonTopTrashed` event and Armor Form snapshot filter, reactive Delay, same-turn activation prohibition, later-turn free optional Armor Form digivolution, and Security Main activation.
- `Security activates the full Main reveal and places the option`: public security attack, Main resolution, matching cards to hand, rest to trash, and option placement.
- `arms Delay from a public Armor Purge battle and free-evolves an Armor Form`: legal BT21-032 Veemon → BT21-035 Flamedramon stacks and two public Armor Purge battles prove the placement-turn refusal, real turn aging, optional source trash, and free evolution into the legal Armor Form card from hand.
- `Delay requires an Armor Form destination`: after aging the Option through a real opponent turn, a public Armor Purge battle trashes BT21-035 while the only candidate in hand is neutral BT1-009; the final state keeps that card in hand, the option in the battle area, and the BT21-032 base in place.

Focused command/results: pending root execution. Expected command: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT21/BT21-094.test.ts --reporter=verbose --no-fsModuleCache`.

Scores pending root gates: implementation 2/2, IR trace 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, rules/evolution proof 2/2, delivery 0/2. These are provisional evidence scores only; root execution and final collection gates remain pending. The prior Armor Purge event seam is covered by the generic `whenDigimonTopTrashed` event implemented by root.

#### Astra top-card-trash and reactive Delay correction

The printed Armor Form top-card trash event is distinct from ordinary digivolution-source trash. A generic `whenDigimonTopTrashed` event now carries the removed top card's identity/controller after promotion, emitted by Armor Purge and De-Digivolve. The IR uses a reactive Delay SubTrigger, paying the Option's trash cost at that event instead of storing an unreachable future payload. Public tests prove same-placement-turn refusal, real turn aging, a second Armor Purge, free legal Armor evolution, and final Option trash. Neutral BT10-055 targets avoid unrelated Megidramon End of Attack effects.

The non-Armor destination negative now also ages the Option through a complete opponent turn and opens a real own Main phase before the public Armor Purge. This isolates the destination trait filter from the placement-turn Delay restriction.

### BT21-095 — Wind Guardians

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact evidence

- Catalog `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`: blue Option, play cost 2, WG, `maxCountInDeck: 4`.
- Printed text: while no face-up security cards, ignore this card’s color requirements. Security/All Turns gives all own WG Digimon Vortex. Main adds the bottom security card to hand, then places this card face up as the bottom security card. Security may play one level-5-or-lower WG Digimon from hand without paying cost.
- KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-095`: Q4609 confirms zero security satisfies the no-face-up condition; Q4610 confirms Main still places the Option face up when security is empty; Q4611–Q4614 cover face-up security state, checks, repeated Security effects, and shuffling.
- Historical comparison: no trusted single-card report was found in this checkout; prior range claims are treated as untrusted pending focused execution.

#### Implementation audit

`apps/api/src/cards/BT21/BT21-095.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-095", compiled)`. It encodes the face-up-security color waiver, Security All Turns Vortex grant to all own WG Digimon, Main bottom-security-to-hand then face-up placement, optional level-5-or-lower WG free play from hand, full coverage, and no residual clauses.

#### Clause-to-test mapping

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Main returns bottom security and places Option face up | `returns the bottom security card and places itself face-up as security` |
| Zero-security Main boundary | `Q4609/Q4610 works with zero security: it only places itself face-up` |
| Security free play of level-5 WG Digimon | `Security plays a level-5 WG Digimon from hand without paying cost` |
| Security target refusal and Vortex grant | `public Security resolution rejects an over-level WG hand candidate`; `Security rejects an over-level hand target (direct timing supplement)`; `publicly grants Vortex to existing and newly played own WG Digimon while face-up in Security` |
| Structural waiver, Main ordering, Security action, and full coverage | `keeps the face-up-security color waiver and security/Main branches faithful` |
| Face-up Security controller boundary | `waives its color only for its controller's face-up-security condition` |
| Public face-up Security Vortex producer and new entrant | `publicly grants Vortex to existing and newly played own WG Digimon while face-up in Security` |
| Security source removal | `loses the Security Vortex grant when the face-up source is checked` |
| Eligible Security refusal | `publicly declines an eligible level-5 WG Security play and leaves the card in hand` |

The Main cases use public `playCard` intents and verify hand/security zones and face-up state. The Vortex case uses public Main placement into face-up Security, then a public play for a new WG entrant, while the source-removal case checks the grant disappears after Security resolution. Security cases verify public level-6 rejection, direct over-level refusal as a supplemental timing check, and public refusal of an eligible level-5 WG play. The exact catalog permits the Security play only from hand; no trash acceptance is claimed. Q4609–Q4614 are recorded as rules boundaries, including zero-security activation, face-up checks, repeated Security effects, and shuffling. No card-local engine change was required.

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. #### Exact source facts

- `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` identifies BT21-096 as a Yellow/Red Option, play cost 4, deck limit 4.
- Printed Main effect: for the turn, one own `[Marcus Damon]` is also treated as a 12000 DP Digimon, cannot digivolve, and gains Rush. Then that Digimon may attack an opponent's Digimon, including an unsuspended Digimon through this effect.
- Printed Security effect: may play one `[Marcus Damon]` from hand or trash without paying, then add this card to hand.
- `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-096` returned Q4615–Q4620 and Q6022–Q6024. These establish dual Tamer/Digimon treatment, temporary overwrite behavior, 0-DP deletion, optional attack, and unaffected-target boundaries. Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-091-100.md`, BT21-096 section; its 8/10 score is untrusted pending current focused execution.

#### Implementation and clause mapping

`BT21-096.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-096", compiled)`. Main binds one exact Marcus Damon Tamer, grants temporary Digimon treatment with base 12000 DP, restricts digivolution, grants Rush and unsuspended-attack permission, then offers the opponent-Digimon attack as optional. Security selects a Marcus Damon from hand/trash for free and returns this Option to hand. Coverage is full and residual is empty.

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Public Main selection, exact play cost, and 12000-DP Digimon treatment | `turns a Marcus Damon into a 12000 DP Rush Digimon and starts its Digimon attack` |
| Cannot digivolve during the turn | same test asserts `isRestricted(..., "digivolve")` |
| Rush and attack of an unsuspended opposing Digimon | same public Main test with unsuspended target |
| Optional attack refusal while grants remain | `publicly declines the optional attack against an unsuspended opponent Digimon`; `retains the temporary Digimon treatment when no opponent Digimon is eligible` |
| Public for-the-turn expiry of DP, Rush, Digimon restriction, and unsuspended-attack permission | `expires every temporary Marcus property after the activating turn ends` |
| Public optional refusal against an eligible unsuspended opponent Digimon, with grants and cost retained | `publicly declines the optional attack against an unsuspended opponent Digimon` |
| Security free Marcus play from hand/trash and add Option to hand | `Security plays Marcus from trash for free, then adds itself to hand`, `Security also plays an eligible Marcus from hand and returns the Option` |
| Exact target binding and full IR coverage | `targets the chosen Marcus permanent and carries the temporary Digimon grants` |

The Main and Security tests use production `playCard` and settled state. The Main assertions record the printed Option play cost (memory 10→6), while the public attack now waits for `combatResolved` and `!observe(engine).isAttacking()` before asserting the unsuspended target deletion. The public refusal test first applies all mandatory grants, then declines the actual optional attack against an eligible unsuspended opponent Digimon and verifies Marcus remains 12000 DP, has Rush and unsuspended-attack permission, cannot digivolve, and leaves the opponent target untouched. The separate no-eligible-target case is named as such and does not claim an eligible refusal. The new lifecycle test runs the activating turn and the opponent's next Main, verifying all four temporary properties have expired. Security covers both printed hand and trash sources with zero memory payment. Q4615–Q4624 boundaries are recorded as rules support; no card-local production correction was required.

### BT21-097 — App Link

Current delivered status: **10/10 — independently verified**. All five ledger categories pass; no outstanding gap. The synchronized 2603-assertion collection/mechanism gate and atomic card proofs are pushed in `81a5f5cc6`; [review PR #4722](https://github.com/vinicius3333/aegis-digimon-tcg/pull/4722) is ready. The collection ledger and final v14 gate supersede historical pending scores and draft findings below.

#### Exact evidence

- Catalog `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`: green Appmon Option, play cost 3, `maxCountInDeck: 4`.
- Printed text: while an Appmon Digimon or Tamer is on the battle area or in breeding, ignore this card’s color requirements. Main reveals the top 3, adds one Appmon/App Driver trait card, trashes the rest, then places this card in the battle area. End of Your Turn grants Delay; Delay may link one hand card to one own Digimon without paying cost. Security places this card in the battle area.
- KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT21-097`: Q4621 rejects a Delay link target without Link; Q4734 confirms battle area or breeding satisfies the Appmon color-waiver field condition.
- Historical comparison: `internal-docs/audits/BT21/BT21-091-102.md` was unavailable in this checkout; historical range claims are treated as untrusted pending focused execution.

#### Implementation audit

`apps/api/src/cards/BT21/BT21-097.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT21-097", compiled)`. It encodes the Appmon field waiver, reveal/add/trash Main sequence, End-of-Your-Turn Delay link with own Digimon recipient and Link-capable hand target, Security placement, full coverage, and no residual clauses.

#### Clause-to-test mapping

| Printed clause or boundary | Named test |
| --- | --- |
| Waiver, reveal/add/rest trash, Delay, Security structural shape | `verifies the Appmon waiver, reveal-and-place Main, Delay Link, and Security placement` |
| Main reveal one qualifying Appmon/App Driver and trash all rest | `reveals an Appmon/App Driver card, trashes the rest, and places itself` |
| Public Main play and placement | same test |
| Appmon waiver from breeding area | `Q4734 waives color for an Appmon Digimon in breeding` |
| End-of-turn Delay public link and recipient | `ages a publicly played Option before activating Delay at a later own turn` |
| Aged eligible Delay Link refusal | `declines an eligible aged Delay link after a public turn boundary` |
| Non-Link card refusal and hand preservation | same Delay test (`noLink`) |
| Aged eligible Delay refusal | `declines an eligible aged Delay link after a public turn boundary` |
| Security placement without memory change | `publicly places itself from Security without changing memory` |

The Main and Delay cases use public play and turn-lifecycle flows, named reveal cards, a legal Link-capable ST22-08, and a non-Link control. The Delay cases prove the same-turn restriction, then age the Option through the opponent's turn and either activate it at a later own end step or explicitly decline an eligible Link; the Link material reaches only an own Digimon, while the non-Link card and opponent recipient remain untouched. The Security case uses a public opponent attack and asserts immediate placement and unchanged memory.

The breeding waiver uses catalog BT21-009 Gatchmon, whose `forms` include `Appmon`; the public Main case separately uses BT21-084 Haru, whose `types` include both Appmon and App Driver. No card-local production correction was required.
